# Project tracking log

This file is the reproducibility record for major implementation, experiment, CI-image, and remote-repository milestones. Do not treat planned or unobserved results as success.

## 2026-09-02 — Codex — live-context correction for immutable Package CI tooling

- **Goal and corrected defect:** bind all shared Package CI tooling to an immutable protected-main commit without trusting a stale pull-request payload base or executing downstream tooling from a synthetic merge. The preceding local design incorrectly assumed `github.workflow_ref` remained `package-ci.yml@refs/heads/main` and that `github.workflow_sha` was itself a protected-main commit for `pull_request`. Live run `33640097553` disproved both assumptions: its authorization environment was `workflow_ref=yinjiayi/openeuler-riscv-packages/.github/workflows/package-ci.yml@refs/pull/1997/merge`, `workflow_sha=event_sha=9b4e79fe75b448ea87d770caf31d900e716dbe41`, and `event_ref=refs/pull/1997/merge`. The synthetic commit has exactly two parents, protected base `223f46fff65da4be98be58f2bd1b8434b82fd07b` followed by exact PR head `dee540b189639304e12abfa86c910fee88c1fec6`.
- **#1997 terminal fact and evidence:** run `33640097553` failed the non-blocking infrastructure Package CI authorization because of the incorrect `@refs/heads/main` assertion. PR #1997 nevertheless merged through its applicable protected infrastructure checks and is closed/merged at squash commit `577a19232e56358e9f940bed56ca7c5af341703d`; this entry does not relabel the failed Package CI run as successful. Read-only API, full job log, and fetched synthetic-commit header evidence are under `/private/tmp/stale-tooling-live-context-fix.7ipSu4/live`; the log and commit-header SHA-256 values are `60964985e6dcf2206fcb79a46c63f5ece1f5fd5e64756d560fe695daa9e19005` and `4de70c13abb3cd756494d9b4838a6854e53cdfd3667ccd7d1a791a24560e1814`.
- **Environment and isolation:** the forward fix uses fresh branch `infra/bind-tooling-live-context-20260902221042` and worktree `/private/tmp/stale-tooling-live-context-fix.7ipSu4/worktree` from exact remote `main` `577a19232e56358e9f940bed56ca7c5af341703d`. Host `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` / `10.213.0.254` was macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64 with 8 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, approximately 40 GiB free disk, Python 3.9.6, Git 2.50.1, and Actionlint 1.7.12. Environment evidence is `/private/tmp/stale-tooling-live-context-fix.7ipSu4/environment.txt`; final generated outputs are isolated under `/private/tmp/stale-tooling-live-context-fix.7ipSu4/validation/full` after `make clean`. No Runner access, target RPM/QEMU/Docker build, workflow rerun, remote mutation, commit, or publication was performed.
- **Corrected trust contract:** the authorization checkout uses two history levels for pull requests, which is sufficient for the synthetic merge commit and both direct parents, while merge-group events retain full history for the protected-base ancestor proof. A pull-request event must provide the exact `refs/pull/<positive PR number>/merge` workflow and event refs, matching full `workflow_sha`, `event_sha`, and checked-out `HEAD`; the commit must have exactly two parents and its second parent must equal the explicit package head. The first parent alone becomes `tooling_sha`. The payload base SHA must be well-formed and is recorded for audit, but may be stale and never selects tooling. A merge-group event instead binds the explicit full `base_sha` from the protected repository/ref only after proving it is an ancestor of the matching checked workflow/event `HEAD`. Push, dispatch, and the fixed protected backfill call retain exact `github.sha` on `refs/heads/main`. All eight tooling checkouts, seven materializers, change-scope base, selector, and CI-state recorder still consume only the one authorization output; package-head identity is unchanged.
- **Validation and next action:** regressions cover the exact #1997 live context shape, stale payload bases, first/second-parent ordering, non-merge and checkout mismatches, wrong PR/ref/repository identities, mutable SHA rejection, merge-group non-ancestor rejection, protected-main events, and consistent downstream consumers. Fresh `make validate`, all 167 unit tests, `make golden`, `make dashboard`, Actionlint, the focused 38-test suite, Python compilation, `git diff --check`, and local authenticated credential guard passed; local results are not RPM/SRPM or publication evidence. Review this uncommitted infrastructure-only correction, then submit it through normal protected checks. Acceptance on the next PR requires authorization to record its synthetic commit separately, emit that commit's first parent as `tooling_sha`, preserve the exact package head as the second parent, and keep all downstream shared tooling on that emitted protected commit.

## 2026-08-31 — Codex — trusted dispatch terminal polling and failure-evidence contract

- **Goal and observed defect:** make `scripts/dispatch-trusted-package-ci` wait for an API-verified terminal workflow instead of trusting `gh run watch`, and preserve a commit/run-bound `--output` record on failure. Run `33350701571` exposed the defect: watch returned while the workflow was still `in_progress`; the one-shot `completed_run` check then failed closed, overwrote all required contexts with error, and the exception path never wrote the requested output file. The acceptance boundary is that an actual workflow failure remains a workflow failure with its real terminal run/job evidence; polling, API, artifact, or binding failures remain fail-closed tool errors and do not manufacture success.
- **Environment, isolation, and inputs:** work used fresh branch/worktree `infra/dispatch-terminal-poll-20260831-c` at `/private/tmp/dispatch-trusted-ci-terminal.PtRYUP/worktree`, initially based on protected `main` `13e152c8e7a4f9456bea416376f62857e9801b26` and finally rebased without conflict onto protected `main` `c1d720bfc0b044870d511aa9e241c75dddba2d3e`, on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` / `10.213.0.254`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, approximately 43 GiB free disk, Python 3.9.6, Git 2.50.1, GitHub CLI 2.97.0, actionlint 1.7.12, and ShellCheck 0.11.0. Final post-rebase validation output was isolated under `/private/tmp/dispatch-trusted-ci-terminal.PtRYUP/validation-postrebase`; `make clean` removed that target before the evidence round. No workflow dispatch, status write, PR/branch mutation, Runner-host operation, local RPM/QEMU/Docker build, or publication was performed.
- **Exact-run diagnosis:** read-only Actions API and `gh run view` snapshots for `33350701571` are under `/private/tmp/dispatch-trusted-ci-terminal.PtRYUP/diagnosis`, with API/view SHA-256 values `7edc2ad721c82b389932bdb41a063c3dbe4e1ff84b95b16b517ae3b122c880d2` and `deb1fc698b1c009ba56142c48646f6adabd3150aa4f432ad2135097beed31450`. They bind workflow title `Package CI PR 1849 6c358fc988d82e667fbdae80e8ddf0eda22c03df`, event `workflow_dispatch`, branch `main`, run attempt 1, and terminal conclusion `failure`. Metadata, source, patch, and merge-policy jobs succeeded; `rpmbuild-riscv64` and `rpm-install-smoke` failed. The repaired poller independently reproduced that truthful terminal classification in `new-poller-run-33350701571.json`, SHA-256 `328d3eff8abc760d6401d2fde89240f81ea50d2fc02c4ebfdf7fa5729bf1fd2b`; it does not diagnose or repair the package failure itself.
- **Implementation and output semantics:** the bridge now polls the exact Actions run API until `completed`, with a three-hour default deadline and per-request 30-second cap. Every observation must retain the exact run ID/title, protected-main branch, and `workflow_dispatch` event; terminal API conclusion, completed view conclusion, and terminal job list must agree. Up to five consecutive transient API/view errors are tolerated, while identity drift fails immediately; both bounds are configurable. `--output` explicitly names one atomic JSON file. After output validation, success, verified workflow failure, and later tool error all record the available PR/base/head binding, run identity, last API observation, polling counters/bounds, job conclusions, status-update outcome, and error. Exit 0 is a bound success, exit 1 is `outcome=workflow-failed`, and exit 2 is `outcome=tool-error`; a completed run failure is never relabeled as a polling error.
- **Validation and limitations:** after the final rebase, fresh `make validate`, all 137 unit tests, `make golden`, `make dashboard`, actionlint, the focused 13-test trusted-dispatch suite, Python compilation, and `git diff --check` passed. The consolidated log is `/private/tmp/dispatch-trusted-ci-terminal.PtRYUP/full-validation-postrebase.log`, SHA-256 `d957cbe0abe9cbb1ae98e2663ca40e0301aa333d0323b6a88827e7767100451c`. Focused regressions cover queued/in-progress/completed sequencing, a bounded transient EOF, the consecutive-error ceiling, deadline exhaustion with last-run evidence, immediate identity rejection, output path rejection, post-dispatch tool-error evidence plus fail-closed statuses, and a terminal workflow failure that stays exit 1 with its failed job. The real run was observed only after it had already completed, so this round does not claim a newly dispatched live long-running canary or altered required statuses.
- **Next action:** publish this as a separate infrastructure PR through normal protected checks. After merge, use one trusted package dispatch whose build is expected to last beyond several polling intervals; require its output file to retain the exact terminal API observation and distinguish workflow failure from tool failure before resuming broader dispatch batches.

## 2026-08-14 — Codex — live Runner fleet verification and machine-readable audit repair

- **Goal and hypothesis:** reconcile the stale claim that the x86_64 Runner fleet was not deployed, verify the five canaries without changing them, and repair any repo-native audit defect separately from package PRs. The acceptance target is that the installed audit entry point exists at the path used by `fleet-audit.py` and that its standard output is machine-only, meaning exactly one JSON document with human-readable preflight diagnostics on standard error.
- **Environment and exact inputs:** local work used clean branch `infra/runner-fleet-audit-runtime` in `/private/tmp/openeuler-runner-audit-fix.UxdJq3LA/worktree`, rebased onto protected `main` `42152f12c99e7a084abbb8afe52b160db4765c6b`. `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` was macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64 with 8 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, 84 GiB free workspace disk, Git 2.50.1, Python 3.9.6, GNU Make 3.81, ShellCheck, and Actionlint. GitHub API readback reported exactly 50 named Runners `.201-.250` online with the reviewed labels; `.215` was busy at the observation instant and the other 49 were idle.
- **Remote checks and trust boundary:** strict SSH used the five previously pinned per-host known-host files under `/private/tmp/runner-canary-deploy.Bg0u7t/install-evidence-v2`; each resolved to ED25519 fingerprint `SHA256:lHG3sACIuiIMoJH/nta2A1amKGy0d3UA7GXeOr68/qk`. Canaries `.201-.205` were Ubuntu 26.04 / Linux 7.0.0-27-generic with 32 CPUs, approximately 60.8 GiB RAM, approximately 1.01 TB free beneath `/opt`, clean `dpkg --audit`, running systemd with no failed units, Docker 29.1.3, QEMU 10.2.1, and Runner 2.336.0. Docker and each Runner service were active; identity, protected-main workflow/event policy, and pinned Runner digest matched. No package, service, policy, registration, or host file was changed during this audit.
- **Observed anomaly and exact evidence:** the documented `fleet-audit.py --stage canary` route failed 5/5 because `/usr/local/libexec/openeuler-actions-runner/audit.sh` did not exist; failed output is isolated under `/private/tmp/runner-canary-reaudit.agA6HJUy`. Running the repository audit from deployed protected-main commit `7439592034a602212b72e511234ce8fda5aeadc0` then passed preflight and all identity, permission, cleanup-lock, Docker, QEMU, registration, policy, and service checks on 5/5 canaries. Per-host raw output, `parsed.json`, and `SHA256SUMS` are under `/private/tmp/runner-canary-reaudit-exact.UULn41Iv`; the parsed 5/5 summary SHA-256 is `544b9bdfd717e2c2799f91d4c43ea98fe74b1e3e71e02ee24ff5ff903d5aa86b`. The raw output also reproduced a second defect: successful preflight text preceded the JSON on stdout, so `json.loads(completed.stdout)` in `fleet-audit.py` could not parse it.
- **Repair, cleanup, and validation:** `install.sh` now installs `audit.sh` into the fixed libexec directory, while `audit.sh` sends successful preflight text to stderr and reserves stdout for its single JSON document. A focused regression binds both sides of that contract. Stale build evidence was not reused: final post-rebase validation started with `make clean` in fresh `/private/tmp/runner-audit-fix-postrebase.YU31VG1Y`; the earlier pre-rebase result remains isolated under `/private/tmp/runner-audit-fix-validation.QJn8qZ1a`. Metadata/repository validation, 102/102 unit tests, 3/3 golden materializations, Bash syntax, error-level ShellCheck, Actionlint, Python compilation, `git diff --check`, and authenticated local credential guard all passed.
- **Limitations and next action:** the repair is locally validated but not yet merged or deployed, so the documented fleet helper remains broken on installed hosts. Publish this infrastructure-only branch through normal protected checks, deploy the exact merged `main` archive idempotently to `.201-.205`, require `fleet-audit.py` to report 5/5 from a fresh evidence directory, then expand the same exact commit to the remaining hosts. No local RPM, Docker, QEMU, or target-architecture package build was run or claimed.

## 2026-08-13 — Codex — auditable official-repository fallback for supplemental outage

- **Goal and acceptance target:** unblock the already published `libstatgrab`, `recutils`, `libffcall`, `ftxui`, `gflags`, and `bmake` package PRs from their common pre-build repository-snapshot failure without treating the outage as package failure. When the fixed supplemental endpoint is unavailable, dependency preparation and installed-RPM smoke may use only the official HTTPS/GPG-checked openEuler 24.03 LTS SP3 `riscv64`/RVA23 repository. Missing dependencies must still fail DNF, while redirects, non-transient HTTP responses, malformed state, mutable URLs, and checksum mismatches remain fail-closed.
- **Environment checks:** local host `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` at `10.213.0.254` is macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64 with 8 CPUs, 16 GiB memory, and approximately 85 GiB free workspace disk; Git and Make are present, while Docker, QEMU, RPM, and rpmbuild are absent. Protected `main` was `5b77ea1069f707b17b1d699320435f4b93dc0f82`; the isolated infrastructure worktree `/private/tmp/openeuler-rpmrepo-fallback.zdMkqH` began clean on branch `infra/rpm-repo-official-fallback`. Runner `10.230.50.201` was read-only checked as Ubuntu 26.04 / Linux 7.0.0-27-generic x86_64 with 32 CPUs, 60 GiB memory, 942 GiB free disk, Docker 29.1.3, QEMU 10.2.1, Runner 2.336.0, and active Docker/Runner services. GitHub readback showed all 50 runners `.201-.250` online and idle with the reviewed labels.
- **Outage evidence and cleanup:** local HTTP requests to `2.27.148.101:38080` first returned an empty reply and later HTTP 503; SSH on public port 38022 closed before key exchange. From `.201`, both `10.20.173.38` ports 22/80 and the public 38022/38080 mappings timed out, and ICMP reported the internal host unreachable. No repository-server command or mutation was possible. The diagnostic was read-only; existing unrelated Runner containers were observed and left untouched. Implementation and generated validation evidence used fresh `/private/tmp/openeuler-fallback-*` paths, and `make clean` ran before evidence-producing validation.
- **Implementation and trust boundary:** `ci/rpm-repo-client.py resolve --allow-unavailable` now distinguishes endpoint unavailability (connection/timeout and HTTP 408, 429, 500, 502, 503, or 504) from response-integrity failures. It atomically records `status=unavailable`, `reason=endpoint-unavailable`, no generation or state digest, an official-only repository allowlist, and a mounted project repo with `enabled=0`. BuildRequires preparation and installed smoke validate the exact evidence/repo key sets and enable `openeuler-riscv-project` only for a verified immutable generation; official-only mode enables only `openeuler-rva23`. Golden evaluation now propagates the same evidence and also passes the previously omitted required package `build_user` policy.
- **Observed results and limitations:** a live fallback exercise at `2026-08-13T11:21:27Z` returned `official-only` with `status=unavailable`; evidence is under `/private/tmp/openeuler-rpmrepo-fallback-live2.J6MwEO`. Commit-bound local gates passed `make validate`, `make golden`, repository validation with the published image required, Actionlint, warning-level ShellCheck, Python compilation, whitespace validation, authenticated credential guard, and 99/99 unit tests. This macOS host cannot produce target RPM evidence, and no package PR is claimed fixed until exact-head GitHub RISC-V CI passes. The supplemental repository remains unavailable and successful protected-main publication will still require service recovery.
- **Next action:** publish this infrastructure-only branch, require its latest-head Package CI, Golden Evaluation, and Auto Merge checks, then merge normally. Update each existing package PR only if its branch has a package-local correction, rerun the exact heads, and classify any official-repository dependency miss explicitly rather than reintroducing the unavailable supplemental source. Restore and independently verify the `/opt` repository service before claiming RPM publication recovery.

## 2026-08-13 — Codex — network-enabled CI policy and upload-first package round

- **Environment and repository:** local host `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` at `10.213.0.254`, Darwin 25.5.0 arm64, 8 logical CPUs, 16 GiB memory, and 88 GiB free workspace disk; Python 3.9.6, Git 2.50.1, and Actionlint 1.7.12. The locked target image remains `sha256:c9d8e675e04cd0ef61c5b2e77d530652db79de51431dc047277cdbd1f63b32b3`; no mutable image or non-RVA23 target was substituted.

Existing unrelated containers and user work were not globally cleaned.
- **Implementation and evidence:** target rpmbuild containers now use Docker `bridge` networking and omit `--offline`; `scripts/build-rpm` retrieves each declared HTTPS source and verifies its committed SHA-256 before invoking `rpmbuild`. The root-only ownership handoff remains `--network none`. Build identity records `network_access_policy=enabled`; golden target builds use the same network policy. New package generation defaults `network_during_build` to true, while historical false values remain schema-valid as statements that the upstream package itself does not require network. Fresh isolated evidence under `/private/tmp/openeuler-networked-ci-rebase.LtfaUj` passed 15/15 focused tests, 89/89 full tests, metadata/repository validation, golden materialization, Actionlint, Python compilation, diff checks, and authenticated credential guard.

The template edit is removed from the next head so the PR remains infrastructure-only. This is a scope-policy defect in the submitted change, not evidence that the networked target build failed.
- **Repository outage and upload state:** the supplemental RPM repository `http://2.27.148.101:38080/state.json` returned an empty reply locally, timed out or returned empty replies from `.201-.204`, and its SSH endpoint did not complete a banner. No repository-server mutation was attempted. GitHub authentication was switched to and read back as repository owner `yinjiayi`; the active credential is never recorded. Package `benchmark` already has open PR #51 and is not duplicated. Prepared `recutils`, `libstatgrab`, `ffcall`, `ftxui`, `gflags`, and `bmake` remain candidate branches/worktrees pending exact six-file static gates and publication.
- **Next action:** publish the repaired infrastructure head, require its latest-head protected checks, then upload each collision-free six-file package branch without local rpmbuild. Diagnose only exact CI artifacts; keep supplemental-repository failures classified as infrastructure until `state.json` is again resolvable.

## 2026-08-08 — Codex — M0 local gate and M1 automation gate

- **Goal and hypothesis:** close the local implementation gate before any remote mutation. Here, the **M0 local gate** means that the six Skills, three fixed golden packages, package metadata, deterministic discovery path, schemas, shared tools, and CI definitions are executable and locally validated. The **M1 automation gate** means that catalog adapters, scheduled all-package update orchestration, sharding, backfill monitoring, repair leasing, Auto-merge policy, and dashboard generation exist and pass isolated tests; it does not mean that 20–50 live packages have already built on GitHub.
- **Target and repository state:** `outputs/openeuler-riscv-packages`, branch `main`, initially uncommitted and with no remote. The target remote still did not exist when this gate began.
- **Environment check:** local macOS host; Apple M1, 8 logical CPUs and 16 GiB installed memory from the initial verified inventory; approximately 5.28 GiB immediately reclaimable memory at the final local check; macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5.0 arm64; 128 GiB free on the workspace volume. Python 3.9.6, Git 2.50.1, GitHub CLI 2.97.0, actionlint 1.7.12, ShellCheck 0.11.0, and Homebrew 6.0.15 were observed. Docker, Podman, QEMU, and `zstd` were not present, so no local OCI/QEMU result is claimed.
- **Cleanup/isolation:** final evidence was produced in the newly allocated `/private/tmp/openeuler-riscv-final-validation.GzRfd9`; `make all` used its `evidence/` child as `VALIDATION_DIR`, and Python bytecode was directed to its `pycache/` child. Existing ignored `work/local-validation` evidence was not reused.
- **Commands executed:** `make all` with the isolated validation directory; six official Skill Creator `quick_validate.py` runs; actionlint; warning-level ShellCheck; `git diff --check`; isolated `compileall`; `--help` probes for all 13 shared executables; full Draft 2020-12 schema-definition and committed-instance validation using isolated `jsonschema`/PyYAML dependencies; two fixed-time discovery runs followed by byte comparison, upstream resolution, package creation, and metadata/schema validation; credential-value scan; and `ci/configure-github.sh --dry-run` with an assertion that no writes were performed.
- **Inputs and versions:** repository contents at the uncommitted M0/M1 gate; the six frozen discovery fixtures dated `2026-08-08T00:00:00Z`; the three committed golden manifests; official Skill Creator under `/Users/askycn/.codex/skills/.system/skill-creator`; isolated `jsonschema` 4.23.0 and PyYAML 6.0.2.
- **Generated outputs:** isolated repository validation, dashboard, golden materialization and canonical fixture archives under `/private/tmp/openeuler-riscv-final-validation.GzRfd9/evidence`; deterministic discovery, resolution, onboarding, and validation evidence under `/private/tmp/openeuler-riscv-final-validation.GzRfd9/forward`.
- **Observed results:** 15/15 unit tests passed. All six Skills passed the official quick validator. All ten schema definitions and all eleven committed package/source/golden instances passed Draft 2020-12 validation. The forward discovery runs were byte-identical; `foo` resolved exactly once at stable version `1.2.3`, retained Arch+AUR lineage, and preserved `foo-bin`/`foo-git` as rejected lineage without executing AUR content; its four generated discovery/onboarding/package/source instances validated. actionlint, warning-level ShellCheck, bytecode compilation, all 13 executable help probes, whitespace validation, credential-value scan, and GitHub configuration dry-run passed. The repository validator emitted only the expected warning that `ci/image.lock` has no published digest and therefore target builds fail closed.
- **Failures/anomalies:** the first Skill validation command omitted the isolated dependency path and failed with missing PyYAML; all six passed after adding the recorded `PYTHONPATH`. An initial actionlint invocation used an invalid positional `never` instead of `-no-color`; the corrected invocation passed. Initial `compileall` attempted to use the sandbox-blocked macOS user cache; the isolated `PYTHONPYCACHEPREFIX` run passed. The first forward metadata validation used singular `--schema-dir`; the documented plural `--schemas-dir` run passed. A broad credential-policy text search intentionally matched documentation that names forbidden secrets; a narrower credential-value scan found no credential literals.
- **Interpretation and limitations:** the local implementation satisfies the M0 local gate and the M1 automation gate. It does not establish a published OCI digest, target-architecture runtime behavior, public Pages availability, remote ruleset enforcement, Auto-merge behavior, repair-in-place behavior, or M1 package-scale acceptance; those require observed GitHub runs. GNU Hello signature verification remains advisory while its official archive SHA-256 is pinned and verified.
- **Next action:** commit the clean local baseline, create and configure the public repository, then observe the CI-image publication and digest-lock PR before exercising the three golden PR paths. Update this log with every remote URL, commit, run, digest, PR state, failure, and repair action.

## 2026-08-08 — Codex — Public bootstrap and first CI-image repair

- **Goal and hypothesis:** create the requested public repository, apply the fail-closed merge/Pages/Actions configuration, and use the first real GitHub-hosted QEMU build to find and repair target-environment defects.
- **Target:** `https://github.com/yinjiayi/openeuler-riscv-packages`, default branch `main`, initial commit `bc9400150f16c95ecca384e6c6daaaffa173454a`; GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-24.04` runner for run `31238329125`.
- **Environment check:** the local publishing host remained a macOS 26.5 arm64 system with 128 GiB workspace space available; the local worktree was clean at `bc940015`. GitHub CLI 2.97.0 authenticated the process-scoped token as `yinjiayi`; the target repository was independently observed absent before creation. The credential value was not written to this repository or to an Actions secret.
- **Cleanup:** the workflow recreated `work/image` and `artifacts/image` before recording evidence. The repair validation used the new isolated directory `/private/tmp/openeuler-riscv-bootstrap-fix.6usHhT`; no prior build result was reused.
- **Commands and remote operations:** `gh repo create ... --public --source=. --push`; independent repository/main-SHA reads; `ci/configure-github.sh --dry-run` then `--apply`; REST reads of repository settings, ruleset, Pages, variables, labels, and Actions secrets; run/PR inspection and failed-log retrieval; an exact manifest-filter regression plus isolated `make all` for the repair.
- **Observed repository state:** the repository is public and the remote `main` SHA matched `bc940015`. Auto-merge, squash-only merging, delete-after-merge, and ruleset `20579949` were active. The ruleset requires the latest head to pass `metadata-validate`, `source-verify`, `rpmbuild-riscv64`, `rpm-install-smoke`, `patch-policy`, and `merge-policy`, with zero required approvals. Six non-secret Actions variables were present and the Actions secret count was zero. GitHub Pages was configured for workflow deployment; the account API reported the existing custom-domain path `http://hbxy.ren/openeuler-riscv-packages/`, which was not yet deployed at this point.
- **First-run results:** CI-image run `31238329125` installed all 279 target packages from the exact configured openEuler repository, then failed the local architecture audit. The manifest contained the RPM database signing-key pseudo-package `gpg-pubkey`, whose architecture is `(none)`; the audit had incorrectly treated it as a foreign payload RPM. PR validation run `31238590096` accepted the repaired manifest and then exposed a separate invalid assertion: the amd64 bootstrap-stage `rpm --root ... -E '%{_arch}'` reports the build host macro, not the target rootfs architecture. That host-stage assertion was removed on branch `codex/fix-bootstrap-host-arch-probe`; the target-platform Containerfile `RUN` still executes `verify-target` under QEMU and requires `%{_arch}=riscv64`. Golden run `31238329124` failed closed because `ci/image.lock` was empty, as designed. Dashboard run `31238329106` began before Pages enablement was applied and failed its Pages metadata read with HTTP 404. Five Dependabot GitHub Actions PRs were created automatically and were left unmerged while the M0 gate was investigated.
- **Repair:** branch `codex/fix-rootfs-gpg-key-arch` changes the architecture audit to permit only the exact `gpg-pubkey`/`(none)` pseudo-record while continuing to reject every other architecture except `riscv64` and `noarch`. A synthetic valid manifest passed, a synthetic `x86_64` payload was rejected, ShellCheck passed, and the isolated repository suite passed 15/15 with only the expected empty-image-lock warning. The first branch push used an unrelated credential cached by the macOS Git helper and was rejected as `wangyf0611`; `gh auth setup-git` switched Git HTTPS credential resolution to the already verified process-scoped `gh` identity, after which the same branch pushed successfully without persisting a repository credential. PR `#6` then exposed that GitHub's Auto-merge mutation requires workflow `contents: write` in addition to `pull-requests: write`; the workflow permission was narrowly corrected on the same PR after run `31238590071` returned `Resource not accessible by integration`.
- **Limitations:** this entry records a locally validated repair, not yet a successful image, GHCR digest, digest-lock merge, golden result, or Pages deployment. The build must be re-observed on GitHub before those states can be claimed.
- **Next action:** open the repair PR, observe its non-publishing QEMU build, merge only after required checks, then dispatch the publishing build from `main` and verify public anonymous GHCR access.

## 2026-08-08 — Codex — QEMU gate, public GHCR push, and digest-lock orchestration repair

- **Goal and hypothesis:** finish the M0 OCI gate, prove public anonymous digest access, and ensure a workflow-created digest-lock PR can obtain the same required checks without a custom secret.

- **Cleanup/isolation:** every image run deleted the prior `work/image` and `artifacts/image` contents before recording environment and build inputs. Local workflow repair validation uses a new temporary directory recorded by its command output and does not reuse the GitHub artifacts as test inputs.
- **Observed QEMU/merge results:** PR `#6` was armed by run `31238678153` after the permission repair and was automatically squash-merged at commit `cc1abbc5aa46f26d7c56930b1939fb68fcd145d6` with all six latest-head required checks successful and zero reviews. PR `#7` non-publishing image run `31238823605` completed in 3m48s: rootfs build, target probe, and RVA23 probe all passed. After updating its head to the latest default branch, all six required checks passed and GitHub Auto-merge squash-merged it as `b30ad6bceb11a512590025789cda5d6703af2356` with zero reviews.
- **Observed GHCR result:** publishing run `31239047167`, fixed to `b30ad6bceb11a512590025789cda5d6703af2356`, rebuilt the image and passed the target/RVA23 probes. It pushed tag `ghcr.io/yinjiayi/openeuler-riscv64-rpmbuild:24.03-lts-sp3-rva23` at digest `sha256:47aed09db0b45ef555465e2476a62b7462c4645992e2055123a0e0839bbba86c`. The public package API reported `visibility=public`; an anonymous GHCR bearer request read that exact digest as a Docker schema-2 manifest with config digest `sha256:bd8ffe5f1a688a380a7e8748020c1183e61cda9dfacf7f1f7333faa75cac2a76` and five layers.
- **Publication anomaly:** after the successful push, the workflow attempted a user-level package visibility PATCH with its repository `GITHUB_TOKEN`; GitHub returned HTTP 404. The same endpoint also returned 404 to the local process token, while the independent public API and anonymous registry read proved that the newly linked package was already public. The redundant mutation caused the run to conclude failure before it could set outputs or create the digest-lock PR; no visibility change is claimed from the failed PATCH.
- **Orchestration repair:** branch `codex/fix-ghcr-publication-gate` replaces the visibility mutation with a public visibility read followed by the anonymous digest manifest gate. It gives only the digest-lock job `actions: write`, adds a read-only `workflow_dispatch(base_sha)` path to Package CI, and dispatches the six required checks on the exact bot-created lock branch before arming native GitHub Auto-merge. This addresses GitHub's rule that a PR created with `GITHUB_TOKEN` does not itself emit a new `pull_request` workflow run.
- **Dependabot anomaly and policy correction:** once Auto-merge became functional, Dependabot PRs `#1`–`#5` passed the infrastructure checks and auto-merged their pinned action updates while the image investigation was running. Attempts to disable the remaining requests arrived after they had already merged. The repair removes `dependabot[bot]` from the Auto-merge allowlist; automatic zero-review merge remains enabled for same-repository maintainer and `github-actions[bot]` PRs defined by this packaging system. The merged action pins remain reviewable in history and are covered by the subsequent Actionlint/static/local and live workflow gates.
- **Current limitation:** the public image exists and is anonymously readable, but `ci/image.lock` is still empty because run `31239047167` failed after the push. Package/golden builds therefore remain intentionally fail-closed until the repaired publication workflow produces and merges a schema/policy-valid digest-lock PR.
- **Next action:** merge the orchestration repair through the six required checks, rerun the publishing workflow from the resulting default-branch SHA, observe the workflow-dispatched lock-PR checks and Auto-merge, then rerun the three golden paths and Pages/update workflows.

The local host remained a macOS 26.5 arm64 system with approximately 128 GiB free before this repair; Docker/QEMU remained unavailable locally.
- **Cleanup/isolation:** remote workflow jobs used fresh GitHub runners and per-package work directories. Downloaded evidence was isolated in the newly allocated `/private/tmp/openeuler-golden-diagnostic.QW33GC`; no older failure artifact or cached build output was used. Local `make all` cleaned and regenerated `work/local-validation` before its 18-test run.
- **Commands and inputs:** protected PR/readback and run inspection; artifact download for run `31242989498`; bounded `rg`/`tail` inspection of `golden-success-hello/rpmbuild-internal.log`; direct inspection of its structured phase result; targeted evaluator regression tests; and `make all`. Input source remained official GNU Hello 2.12.3 with pinned SHA-256 `0d5f60154382fee10b114a1c34e785d8b1f492073ae2d3a6f7b147687b366aa0`.
- **Observed protected merge and routing:** PR #16 passed the six required latest-head checks and was automatically squash-merged with zero reviews. In run `31242989498`, the native-only kernel-module route passed without claiming a build, and the inline-assembly baseline again produced its expected RISC-V-specific failure and `repair-queued` state.
- **Observed Hello result:** source verification, locked-image target/RVA23 probes, and dependency preparation succeeded. The actual offline build ran for 496.694 seconds. All seven upstream tests executed with 6 PASS, 1 SKIP, 0 FAIL, and 0 ERROR. RPM packaging then failed because GNU install generated `/usr/share/info/dir`, which was installed but not declared in the package. The corrected first effective error is `error: Installed (but unpackaged) file(s) found:`; this is a SPEC packaging defect, not a QEMU or upstream-test failure.
- **Repair:** the evidence-backed package repair is to remove only `%{buildroot}%{_infodir}/dir`, the generated system-wide Info index owned by `install-info`, without skipping a test or adding a RISC-V patch. Repository change-scope policy requires that SPEC edit to be delivered in a separate package-only PR after this shared-tool repair. The evaluator now requires exactly one final `job_id`/`checks` build envelope per golden package, derives build status only from that envelope, treats fixture archives and official sources as distinct evidence paths, and requires an exact URL/SHA-256/`verified=true` source-verification record for the official Hello archive. A negative regression proves that an unrelated `{status: passed}` document cannot overrule a failed final envelope. Strict replay also showed that the classifier preferred RPM's generic outer `Bad exit status` over the inline fixture's explicit `x86-only ... lacks a RISC-V implementation`; specific RISC-V evidence now precedes the generic SPEC-wrapper fallback, with a combined outer-result/inner-log regression.
- **Local result and limitation:** isolated evidence `/private/tmp/openeuler-golden-classifier-fix.g9hqB8` passed 19/19 tests, metadata and static repository validation with no errors or warnings, deterministic golden materialization, dashboard generation, Actionlint, warning-level ShellCheck, and whitespace checks. Reclassifying the retained real inline-assembly phase plus internal log now returns `riscv-specific` and `repair-queued`. This entry does not yet claim a successful rebuilt Hello RPM, installation smoke test, or three-of-three remote golden evaluation; those require the next protected PR run.
- **Next action:** merge the shared evaluator/classifier repair through protected checks, then create a package-only GNU Hello PR with the recorded `%{_infodir}/dir` repair and require a real successful build/install/smoke plus strict three-package evaluation before closing this gate.

## 2026-08-08 — Codex — Fail-closed M1 discovery evidence gate

- **Goal and finding:** audit the first live primary discovery snapshot against DISC-006 before selecting the M1 cohort. The immutable snapshot from run `31240886438` contains 100 entries labeled eligible, but zero has both an HTTPS upstream release archive and a full SHA-256; three also contain explicit prerelease labels. Distribution presence, package-file checksums, and catalog metadata digests do not prove official upstream stable release bytes.
- **Target, environment, and cleanup:** independent worktree `/private/tmp/openeuler-strict-discovery`, branch `infra/strict-discovery-evidence`, initially based on protected `main` `f732e93` and then merged with `e06e180`; local host/tool state is the same as above. Fresh evidence directories were `/private/tmp/openeuler-strict-validation.LG0OE7`, `/private/tmp/openeuler-strict-postmerge.4GC6ip`, and `/private/tmp/openeuler-contract-postfix.ofdXia`; no old generated result was reused.
- **Policy definition and change:** a **verified release source** is an HTTPS official stable release/tag archive whose exact bytes are pinned by a full 64-hex SHA-256; distribution package checksums and metadata digests are not substitutes. Discovery now excludes upstream versions labeled alpha, beta, rc, pre/preview, dev, nightly, or snapshot; ranks verified byte evidence before unverified higher version text during component deduplication; retains all distribution lineage; and moves any component without verified bytes to `unverified-upstream`. `resolve-upstream` independently re-enforces HTTPS plus SHA-256, so a hand-crafted or historical eligible flag cannot bypass the gate. The snapshot policy records `verified_source_required=true`; old immutable snapshots remain schema-readable but must be re-resolved.
- **Validation:** a regression makes an unverified AUR record claim version `9.9.9` while verified Arch evidence pins `1.2.3`; the resolved candidate remains `1.2.3` and retains both lineages. Further regressions reject a checksum-pinned `2.0rc1`, a stable-looking source without a checksum, binary/VCS/stale/testing inputs, and a direct resolver input lacking checksum. Fresh full validation passed 23/23 tests, metadata/static checks, deterministic golden materialization, dashboard generation, Actionlint, isolated compilation, whitespace checks, and the official Skill Creator validator for the changed discovery Skill. All 10 schemas pass Draft 2020-12 definition checks; both the historical and new fixture snapshots validate. Two fixed-input discovery runs were byte-identical. Re-resolving the historical live snapshot now produces zero components and 119 explicit evidence rejections rather than unsafe onboarding candidates.
- **Interpretation and M1 boundary:** the control plane supports primary and supplemental catalog adapters, immutable snapshots, one-package onboarding/update PRs, daily sharding/idempotency/backfill monitoring, QEMU CI, local repair, Auto-merge, and Pages deployment. It does not yet satisfy the M1 scale gate of 20–50 representative packages or AT-04's five real candidate PRs. Raw Arch/AUR metadata cannot safely supply the required official upstream source checksum, and the policy forbids trusting or executing AUR recipes to obtain it. The next milestone is a reviewed official-upstream enrichment stage, followed by five independent onboarding PRs and then a 20–50 package cohort; do not weaken this evidence gate to manufacture candidate counts.

The immutable input was `discovery-20260808T074348Z-31246813686.json` / snapshot ID `discovery-20260808T074358Z-0e0d27c35009b43d`. No AUR recipe or source-tree instruction was executed. Official archives were downloaded with bounded size/retry controls, checked for absolute paths, parent traversal, and symlinks, and then inspected only from isolated extraction directories.
- **Reviewed cohort:** `abduco` 0.6 (`c90909e13fa95770b5afc3b59f311b3d3d2fdfae23f9569fa4f96a3e192a35f4`), `dvtm` 0.15 (`8f2015c05e2ad82f12ae4cf12b363d34f527a4bbc8c369667f239e4542e1e510`), BearSSL 0.6 (`6705bba1714961b41a728dfc5debbe348d2966c117649392f8c8139efc83ff14`), cabextract 1.11 (`b5546db1155e4c718ff3d4b278573604f30dd64c3c5bfd4657cd089b823a3ac6`), and bftpd 6.7 (`40317957c3667fded7541ff4b0f44328573827ef0315519a44ca1b627f5eafb2`). bftpd intentionally advances the frozen Arch observation from 6.6 to the official stable 6.7 release. A calcurse 4.8.2 candidate was dropped after both official-hostname variants timed out during TLS handshakes; no mirror was substituted for official evidence.
- **Implementation:** `catalog/upstream-releases.yaml` and its Draft 2020-12 schema bind reviewed evidence to the immutable snapshot. `resolve-upstream --reviewed-evidence` can promote only a visible component, requires stable HTTPS release/source endpoints, a full lowercase SHA-256, license and archive-safety evidence, rejects version regressions, and never mutates the snapshot. `check-update` now implements the previously declared `release_regex`/`source_url_template` contract for bounded official HTML/XML indexes and prefers reviewed release assets over generic forge tarballs. `create-package` preserves the source snapshot ID. Metadata validation now checks the evidence registry and its committed snapshot binding. README and the discovery Skill document the executable flow.
- **Package definitions:** five new `packages/<id>/` trees contain pinned sources, real SPEC build/install/check sections, smoke tests, empty local patch series, provenance, and daily-update configuration. abduco and dvtm use their upstream Makefiles with complete compiler feature defines; dvtm stages terminfo. BearSSL builds and tests the static library and `brssl`, with a `bearssl-devel` subpackage. cabextract uses its bundled libmspack and upstream test suite. bftpd disables optional PAM/zlib and installs only the daemon, manual, and protected configuration instead of creating runtime log/state files in the RPM payload.
- **Observed validation:** targeted discovery/update regressions passed 8/8 and the full suite passed 25/25. All five source manifests reverified offline against the exact downloaded bytes; package metadata and patch policy passed 5/5. A clean `make all` passed metadata/static validation, tests, deterministic golden materialization, and dashboard generation for eight managed/golden rows. All six Skill Creator validators passed. Full Draft 2020-12 checking passed 11 schema definitions and 23 committed/generated instances with zero errors; Actionlint, warning-level ShellCheck, and `git diff --check` passed. The first real update scan exposed anonymous GitHub API HTTP 403 for abduco/dvtm while the other three endpoints passed; switching those two checks to their official project release pages produced a fresh live result of `no-update=5`, failures 0, at the expected versions.
- **Evidence boundary and remote blocker:** local evidence proves schemas, policy, exact source bytes, update resolution, and package definitions; it does not prove any of the five SPECs build or smoke successfully on RISC-V. Those claims require the locked OCI and GitHub-hosted QEMU jobs. GitHub CLI 2.97.0 is installed but `gh auth status` reports no authenticated host. In accordance with the publish Skill, no branch, commit, push, PR, label, Auto-merge, dashboard deployment, or RPM-build result was fabricated. The previously pasted PAT must remain revoked and was not reused.
- **Next action:** authenticate interactively with `gh auth login`, then publish the shared resolver/schema/tests/docs/registry work as one infrastructure PR. After protected merge, publish exactly five package-only branches/PRs, observe each latest-head source/build/install/smoke result, repair only the same trusted PR branch when structured evidence warrants it, and let zero-review Auto-merge merge only passing heads. Update this record with PR numbers, exact commits, Actions runs, artifacts, classifications, and final merge state before expanding to 20–50 packages.

## 2026-08-09 — Codex — Full six-distribution catalog and 20-package M1 cohort

- **Goal and hypothesis:** expand beyond the initial five packages by resolving all configured stable distribution indexes, preserving every decision, and preparing a 20-package representative cohort only where independent official-upstream evidence is sufficient. “All catalog records” means full accountable discovery coverage; it does not mean that an unverified distribution entry is safe to turn into an RPM or PR.

Failed or superseded full snapshots were moved to `/private/tmp/openeuler-full-catalog.ku6AU1` rather than reused. Determinism was checked with an independently regenerated gzip file.
- **Discovery inputs:** official Arch stable `core`/`extra` 15,163 records, AUR metadata 117,191, openSUSE Tumbleweed 17,113, Fedora 44 GA 23,660, Debian 13.6 `stable` (`trixie`) 38,068, and Ubuntu 26.04 LTS GA (`resolute`) 40,311; total 251,506 raw records. Rawhide, testing/unstable, staging, multilib, proposed/development/pre-release channels, AUR pure `-bin`, stale AUR entries, and VCS-only entries were excluded by recorded policy. No AUR recipe or other distribution packaging content was executed.
- **Snapshot and normalization result:** immutable snapshot `discovery-20260808T165000Z-9a89920c269462cd.json.gz` uses normalization version 5 and records every official metadata URL/object checksum plus each normalized-input checksum. It contains zero directly importable candidates and 181,134 deduplicated rejection/hold decisions: 89,975 `unverified-upstream`, 46,870 `stale`, 17,752 `license-blocked`, 12,949 `vcs-only`, 12,765 `binary-only`, and 823 `pre-release`. Two complete fixed-time runs were byte-identical at gzip SHA-256 `bd79d0cd34f3d674c10736aa83d8f9f78f35531ae99cef53663b62bc74458fe0`.
- **Reviewed cohort:** 20 official tag/release archives were downloaded with bounded retries, size limits, path/link inspection, license-text inspection, and calculated SHA-256 values recorded in `catalog/upstream-releases.yaml`. The resolver produced exactly 20 unique components: 10 with Arch lineage, four with AUR plus Debian/Fedora/openSUSE/Ubuntu lineage, and six with four supplementary-distribution lineages. Nineteen are cross-distribution corroborated; `bftpd` is explicitly Arch-only at the distribution layer and relies on separately verified official upstream bytes.
- **Package implementation:** each of the 20 directories has a real source manifest, SPEC, build/install/check logic, smoke test, provenance, and daily stable-release metadata. The 15 added packages are `cmatrix`, `nyancat`, `picojson`, `reproc`, `zxcvbn-c`, `macchanger`, `sysfsutils`, `libfastjson`, `nudoku`, `xbanish`, `dragonbox`, `bitwise`, `checksec`, `sipsak`, and `stressapptest`. `macchanger` carries one checksum-recorded downstream build-system patch for an absent, unused `doc/gpl.texi` declaration; it is not a RISC-V source workaround and no upstream write was made.
- **Source and dependency evidence:** the repository's own `build-rpm --verify-only` path independently returned `source-verified` for all 20 official source URLs. Offline `--plan` returned `planned` for all 20 and extracted 21 unique BuildRequires capabilities. A read-only query of the exact target repository verified `repomd.xml` SHA-256 `1e7269d6fa08e8f837e0ead13ad324e7f4ee5569dde6691378a61a806145bc14`, primary SQLite SHA-256 `7023e5bb09a3a268acc6a411801bfabaf282d8569371f24cd63ede54f6c1dfbc`, and all 21 capabilities; `pkgconfig` resolves through `pkgconf`.
- **Observed local validation:** metadata validation passed all 23 managed/golden package trees. `make all` passed static validation, 25/25 tests, deterministic golden materialization, and dashboard generation. Draft 2020-12 validation with jsonschema 4.25.1 passed all 11 schemas and 55 committed instances. Actionlint, warning-level ShellCheck, `git diff --check`, credential-literal scanning, and all six official Skill Creator validators passed.

One wrapper used zsh's read-only `status` variable and was rerun with a task-specific name. Two Skill validation attempts lacked PyYAML before the recorded Python 3.12/PyYAML environment passed. An initial plan probe used an empty source cache in a network-restricted sandbox and stopped at source verification; the fresh offline run reused only the independently verified cache and passed all 20. Tests are not disabled or weakened for either package.

The pasted PAT was not placed in a command, file, log, commit, cache, Actions secret, or artifact; the connected GitHub application will be used for scoped writes. ## 2026-08-09 — Codex — Update-source contract and license-evidence correction

- **Target and environment:** public repository `yinjiayi/openeuler-riscv-packages`, infrastructure branch based on main commit `65a2f2c8ccbd3d2807083e0ca582f3d5769b2ab7`; local macOS 26.5 arm64 host with approximately 127 GiB free disk, Git 2.50.1, Python 3.9.6, actionlint 1.7.12, and no local Docker/QEMU/RPM runtime. - **Cleanup and inputs:** fresh validation output `/private/tmp/openeuler-infra-review.PlCOhL`; fresh archive-audit extraction `/private/tmp/openeuler-review-audit.Tu1R4X`; previously checksum-verified official bitwise, cabextract, CMatrix, sipsak, sysfsutils, and zxcvbn-c archives were used read-only. No AUR or distribution recipe was executed.
- **Implementation:** `check-update apply` now rewrites exactly one `Source0` to the verified manifest basename and fails closed for a missing, empty, or path-containing filename. Its forward test proves an update from `demo-1.0.tar.gz` to the differently named `upstream-2.tar.gz`. Reviewed evidence now records bitwise's compiled BSD-2-Clause files, cabextract's GPL/LGPL source combination, CMatrix's required `kbd` runtime for documented console-font mode, and sipsak's `c-ares-devel` requirement for DNS SRV support.
- **Observed evidence:** official bitwise source headers explicitly reference the bundled BSD-2-Clause `LICENSE`; cabextract builds bundled LGPL-2.1-only libmspack and retains LGPL-2.0-or-later GNU portability source; CMatrix source documents `-l` and calls `setfont matrix`; the exact openEuler RVA23 primary SQLite records `kbd` and `/usr/bin/setfont`; sipsak's configure logic enables SRV only through `libcares`, and the exact target repository records `c-ares-devel` 1.19.1 for `riscv64`.
- **Validation:** isolated `make all` passed repository/static validation, all 25 tests, golden evaluation, and dashboard generation. JSON parsing, actionlint, Python bytecode compilation, and `git diff --check` passed. An attempted ShellCheck invocation on the Python executable correctly returned SC1071 and is not counted as validation; Python compilation replaced that in the successful check set.
- **Limitations and next action:** this change corrects shared contracts and evidence but does not itself prove package RPM behavior. Publish it as a separate protected infrastructure PR, observe its latest-head checks and automatic merge, then update each affected package PR with package-local fixes and rerun the immutable RISC-V OCI gates. The exposed PAT remains unused and must be revoked.

## 2026-08-10 — Codex — Process-scoped GitHub token Skill contract

- **Goal and policy definition:** make explicit that a user-authorized GitHub token may authenticate local `gh`/GitHub operations during the current process. A **process-scoped token** is available only through the running process environment or secret-safe stdin injection; it is not a repository or Actions secret. Its value is forbidden from command arguments, repository files, staged/committed content, PR text/comments, logs, artifacts, Actions secrets/variables, Pages output, or another public surface.
- **Implementation:** added executable `scripts/github-credential-guard`. It accepts no token argument, reads only `GH_TOKEN`, rejects GitHub Actions in local-only mode, validates `gh` identity without exposing stderr/token data, checks the active value against tracked/untracked non-ignored files, the staged binary diff, and configured publication paths, and emits a value-free structured preflight. The repair, bootstrap, onboarding, update, and dashboard Skills now explicitly allow process-token use and require the guard before remote mutation/read and again before commit/push where applicable. Repository instructions, README policy, `.env` ignores, and static repository validation were synchronized.
- **Security regression evidence:** a generated in-test token successfully authenticated through a fake local `gh`; neither stdout, stderr, nor the JSON preflight contained its value. Placing the same generated value in untracked Pages output or a staged file made the guard fail while reporting only path names. Static validation detects legacy and fine-grained GitHub token-shaped literals without embedding a credential in the tests.
- **Validation and anomaly:** the first official Skill Creator validation attempts did not execute because both available Python runtimes lacked `PyYAML`; they are not counted as passes. PyYAML 6.0.2 was installed only in `/private/tmp/openeuler-skill-validator-deps`, after which all five changed Skills returned `Skill is valid!`. Fresh isolated validation at `/private/tmp/openeuler-token-skill-validation-final.0bF1wh` passed 27/27 tests, metadata validation, repository policy with zero errors/warnings, deterministic golden materialization, dashboard generation, bytecode compilation, and whitespace checks.
- **Remote boundary and next action:** no GitHub branch, commit, PR, label, check, secret, variable, Pages resource, or repository setting changed in this update. After the revised Skill is published through the normal protected infrastructure PR, use an authorized process credential to resume the strict package queue, rerunning the guard before every commit/push and preserving the existing latest-head/lease rules.

- **Commands and evidence route:** live PR state was read with `gh pr list`; the trusted queue was regenerated with `scripts/watch-failed-prs`; leases, exact-head guards, and releases used `scripts/claim-repair`; exact CI archives came from the recorded run IDs; changed packages passed `scripts/build-rpm --verify-only --offline`, package-scoped `scripts/validate-metadata`, repository validation, `git diff --check`, and full Draft 2020-12 instance validation before the same-branch push. The credential guard ran before remote mutations; no credential value entered an argument, file, log, commit, PR, artifact, workflow setting, or Pages output.

Protected `main` contains 60 of them plus the three fixed golden fixtures, and 15 reviewed components have open independent onboarding PRs. The union is exactly 75/75, with zero missing components, zero unreviewed package PRs, and zero unexpected non-golden package directories. Across those 75 component records, preserved immutable discovery lineage covers Arch `core` for 13 components, Arch `extra` for 51, AUR metadata for 47, Debian stable for 71, Fedora GA for 71, openSUSE Tumbleweed for 71, and Ubuntu GA standard-support components for 54; counts overlap because one upstream component is intentionally corroborated by several distributions before de-duplication.
- **CLI11 repair:** PR `#63` moved from head `0f1e987ca7172fc6ac358dbaf96cfb6a4563290e` to `8cac5fec4c3301e23000e4f0b9afc7548fcedd94`. Marking a header-only result `noarch` changed RPM's VPATH directory name; the hard-coded `riscv64-openEuler-linux-gnu` staging path therefore hid the pinned Catch2 fixture and CMake attempted a forbidden network download. The SPEC now stages the verified fixture under `%{_vpath_builddir}`. Offline source, metadata, repository, and schema checks passed before push; the new target run remains the authority for RPM success.
- **doctest infrastructure rerun:** PR `#64` did not enter `rpmbuild`; run `31398894496` failed while the official openEuler repository repeatedly returned HTTP/2 stream errors for `glibc-devel`. The package tree remained unchanged, the lease was released with `requeue`, and only the failed jobs were rerun. No infrastructure download failure was disguised as a package patch.
- **libseccomp native boundary:** PR `#92` moved from head `475d46e40bfb01f9d3b38d72f526a3d65c887dd6` to `655d27dbcd46dd42ae68989da7229fc2e467d1c7`. Its QEMU job executed the upstream C regression batches: simulator cases passed, while `52-basic-load` returned `ECANCELED` from the path that calls `seccomp_load`; the subsequent Python-mode repetition reached the two-hour job limit. Because loading filters exercises the target kernel and QEMU User shares/emulates host-kernel syscall behavior, the package is now truthfully `needs-native-riscv` with `riscv_status=qemu-limitation`. The complete upstream `%check` remains in the SPEC; tests were not removed or falsely marked successful.
- **Remote infrastructure result:** the preceding ruleset/tracking PR `#109` passed protected checks and normally Auto-merged as `f26b2ec179cbc8806d207d88fedbb59acd270158`, recording the live non-strict required-check rule used for parallel package Auto-merge. No admin merge or protection bypass was used.
- **Limitations and interpretation:** snapshot `discovery-20260808T165000Z-9a89920c269462cd` contains 181,134 raw distribution/AUR records but zero directly importable candidates because those indexes alone do not establish a verified official stable archive and SHA-256. Those fail-closed records are a discovery backlog, not 181,134 build-ready packages, and are not counted as submitted. CI still running at this checkpoint is not claimed as passed; native-only packages remain deliberately open without a self-hosted runner.
- **Next action:** allow already-started independent package runs to finish and repair only newly queued exact-head failures. In parallel, expand the reviewed registry by enriching held records with official upstream stable-release URLs, checksums, archive/license inspection, target dependency availability, and normalized cross-distribution lineage; only then create one new package PR per newly eligible component.

Installed versions are Nginx `1.28.3-2ubuntu1.8`, `createrepo-c` `1.2.1-2build1`, RPM `6.0.1-1build1`, rsync `3.4.1+ds1-7ubuntu0.3`, and OpenSSH server `10.2p1-2ubuntu3.5`. Only passing main/reusable runs stage regular RPM/SRPM files, bind them to commit/run/SHA-256, upload payload before `.ready`, and poll the generation-specific public state. ## 2026-08-12 — Codex — Non-recursive user-connector backfill event

- **Observed anomaly:** PR `#186` passed its required checks and Auto Merge Policy, then GitHub normally squash-merged it as `a6cd7d085b1f8ee20b770e0b4556712a0302ceff`. The expected marker-matched backfill did not start: the workflow run API returned an exact total of zero runs. GitHub suppresses new workflow runs for events produced by repository `GITHUB_TOKEN` activity to prevent recursive workflow execution; the auto-merge had been armed by that token. This is an event-origin boundary, not a path-filter or package-plan failure.
- **Corrective route and safety:** update only the marker plus this record in a draft PR, run the normal latest-head protected checks, then mark it ready and merge it through the authenticated GitHub user connector with the exact head guard. This is a normal branch-protected merge, not an admin merge or ruleset bypass. Its main push is not produced by an Actions token, so the already-merged path filter can start the one full backfill. The marker remains inert on unrelated future commits.
- **Acceptance target:** confirm one backfill run whose `head_sha` equals the resulting protected-main merge, download and validate its immutable plan, monitor every matrix child to terminal state, and independently verify the public `/opt` repository generation and RPM/SRPM counts. Do not count skipped native/retired/golden packages or failed builds as published.

## 2026-08-12 — Codex — Reusable-workflow permission ceiling repair

- **Observed startup evidence:** the connector merge of PR `#187` produced exact protected-main SHA `d5ca14cf39f66d65bd6d2902f973c53975359646` and correctly started RPM Repository Backfill run `31562351527` as actor `yinjiayi`. GitHub terminated it immediately with `startup_failure`: zero jobs, zero check runs, and zero artifacts. No package build, secret use, rsync upload, or remote repository mutation occurred in that run.
- **Root cause:** the backfill caller granted only `contents: read`, while its reusable `package-ci.yml` contains a PR-state job whose declared ceiling also includes `actions: read`, `issues: write`, and `pull-requests: write`. GitHub validates reusable-workflow permission ceilings before evaluating job conditions, so the fact that the PR-state job is skipped during backfill does not make the narrower caller valid.
- **Repair and security boundary:** align the caller ceiling with the called workflow. Individual called jobs still use the Package CI workflow's own top-level or job-local permission declarations; the build, source, smoke, and upload jobs remain read-only. The write-capable PR-state job remains conditionally skipped for a protected-main backfill. Add a dependency-free regression that parses every permissions block and fails if the caller ceiling is lower than any reusable-workflow job request.
- **Retry route:** update the exact marker in the same protected infrastructure PR, run the full local and latest-head gates, and repeat the draft-to-ready connector squash merge with an exact head guard. Accept only a non-startup-failure backfill whose plan job runs and whose head SHA equals the resulting main commit.

## 2026-08-12 — Codex — Backfill download resilience after exact dependency-stage failure

- **Goal and acceptance target:** preserve parallel package backfill while preventing a transient failure from the single official openEuler mirror from being misclassified as an `argparse` source or SPEC defect. Dependency acquisition may retry in the same ephemeral container and reuse its DNF cache; RPM construction remains offline, upstream checks remain unchanged, and deterministic missing dependencies still fail after a finite bound.
- **Environment and exact evidence:** local validation ran on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 logical CPUs, 106 GiB free workspace disk, and Python 3.9.6. The remote operation is RPM Repository Backfill run `31562621344` at protected-main SHA `6e0921f6d2f1253dc2c3b7afe8fcb6140e92d92a`. Its immutable 112-package plan artifact SHA-256 is `4d2c47a9754fd4b8ea13180acbf0ad45339a132f5ce3435c88d118e92be35270`. The exact `argparse` build artifact `9128514811` was downloaded into fresh `/private/tmp`, passed ZIP integrity checks, and locally hashed to `73f7276588be4badec310f3382c2aecdf5288a82505a05f8c713f389db4e0c8f`, exactly matching GitHub's artifact digest.
- **Observed first error and interpretation:** source, metadata, patch, merge-policy, and immutable repository gates all passed. Dependency installation then downloaded part of the 121-package transaction before the official openEuler endpoint returned HTTP/2 stream errors and low-speed timeouts for `gcc-c++`; DNF exhausted the only enabled official mirror and exited before `rpmbuild`. The structured evidence correctly classified this as unknown rather than authorizing a package patch. `argparse` is unchanged, and its repository publication was skipped.
- **Implementation:** `prepare-build-deps.py` now uses three bounded attempts in the same container, records the successful attempt count, retains already downloaded packages between attempts, and explicitly configures 20 per-file retries, a 60-second transfer timeout, a one-byte/s low-speed floor, and one parallel download. Backfill remains parallel at eight QEMU builds, reducing repeated large dependency transactions against the same endpoint without reverting to serial execution. Static validation and two unit regressions enforce the retry and concurrency contracts.
- **Cleanup, validation, and anomaly:** work used the isolated clean worktree `work/infra-dnf-retry`; `make clean` removed prior generated validation before each full run. The first full validation correctly failed because static policy still required the former `max-parallel: 20`; that stale assertion was updated and is not counted as a pass. The fresh run then passed metadata/repository validation, dashboard generation, all three golden fixtures, and 48/48 tests. Actionlint returned no findings, Python compilation passed with an isolated `/private/tmp` bytecode cache, credential guard reported `authentication_mode=none` and `token_value_recorded=false`, and `git diff --check` passed. The local host has no Docker/QEMU target environment, so only the next exact-head Actions run can validate network behavior and RISC-V builds.
- **Next action:** publish this as a separate protected infrastructure PR. Let the current fail-fast-disabled matrix continue so successful packages still enter the `/opt` repository, then trigger a new protected-main backfill with the resilience change. Because the publisher is checksum-bound and idempotently accepts an existing filename only when its bytes match, successful packages can be rebuilt without increasing canonical RPM counts; previously transient failures should then be retried under the new policy.

## 2026-08-12 — Codex — Authoritative SRPM header classification and quarantined-batch recovery

- **Observed server evidence:** after several packages passed target build and installed smoke, their rsync payload and `.ready` marker reached `/opt/openeuler-riscv-rpm-repo/incoming`, but each generation remained HTTP 404 and the public state stayed at the empty bootstrap. A fresh remote check reconfirmed host `jiayi-github-repo` / `10.20.173.38`, 32 CPUs, 60 GiB memory with 59 GiB available, and `/opt` on the 2.0 TiB filesystem with about 2.0 TiB free. Nginx, the systemd path watcher, and timer were active. Journal evidence showed deterministic quarantine with `ready-marker kind does not match RPM architecture`; this is a server validation bug, not an RPM build or rsync failure.
- **Root cause:** the upload stager correctly classifies files found under `SRPMS` as source. The publisher incorrectly inferred the received RPM kind from `%{ARCH}`. Exact built SRPM inspection showed `SOURCEPACKAGE=1` and `SOURCERPM=(none)`, while `%{ARCH}` retained the package build architecture (`noarch` for the inspected checksec SRPM and potentially `riscv64` for others). The corresponding binary RPM had `SOURCEPACKAGE=(none)`. RPM's `SOURCEPACKAGE` header tag is therefore the authoritative discriminator; filename suffix and build architecture are not substituted for it.
- **Repair and recovery boundary:** query and validate `%{SOURCEPACKAGE}`, accept only `1` or `(none)`, and derive source/binary kind from that tag while retaining the existing architecture allowlist, header identity, size, SHA-256, and exact ready-marker checks. Two regressions cover a noarch SRPM and noarch binary. Quarantined batches are preserved; only directories whose structured failure reason exactly matches this now-corrected validation defect may be requeued, with their failure records retained separately. Other quarantines remain untouched. The deployed publisher must match the reviewed repository file byte-for-byte before the service is restarted.
- **Observed recovery result:** infrastructure PR #189 exact publisher bytes SHA-256 `8efdf7974d8a0e3dd73e1e8b76c66ac65bdd8e02daa53a6ed91c5b74233bd3a4` were copied to a temporary server path, rehashed, installed byte-for-byte as `/usr/local/sbin/openeuler-rpmrepo-publish`, compiled, and the temporary copy removed. The watcher and timer were stopped during the replacement. Thirteen quarantines with the exact matching reason were requeued; their original `failure.json` records were moved to root-only `failed-evidence`, not deleted. Together with one concurrently arrived valid batch, the corrected service published 14 package generations successfully, left zero ready batches and zero remaining quarantine records, then returned inactive/success while the watcher and timer remained active.
- **Public verification:** latest public generation `dragonbox-6e0921f6d2f1253dc2c3b7afe8fcb6140e92d92a-31562621344-1` reports 44 binary RPMs and 14 SRPMs. Fresh public downloads of binary and source `repomd.xml` hashed to `5c43f1b447c096868cf31e03200b09c8a374f88dfbefa8061bdcd397f75efd75` and `7b1bd6abd2ae076c90b751554bca74a9af78760386965cdd9dc272d584cb46f2`, exactly matching `state.json` and the server-side files. A successful server publish does not erase the separate transient `argparse` dependency-stage failure; that package remains for the queued resilient backfill.
- **Next action:** require infrastructure PR #189's updated exact head to pass protected checks, merge it normally through the user connector so the resilience marker emits a non-recursive protected-main event, and monitor both the current and queued backfills to terminal evidence. Continue comparing cumulative public RPM/SRPM counts and record genuine package failures separately.

## 2026-08-12 — Codex — Sixteen-way hosted QEMU backfill

- **Goal and acceptance target:** increase package throughput without weakening source, metadata, checksum, offline-build, upstream-test, or installed-smoke gates. The repository-controlled backfill matrix must admit sixteen independent QEMU package calls; GitHub's account-level hosted-runner quota remains an external queue ceiling and is not represented as a guaranteed capacity.
- **Environment and observed baseline:** the isolated worktree `work/infra-backfill-concurrency-16` starts from protected main `1443971c92a43d353fb0662d3b7c3f476e71a998` on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 logical CPUs, and 103 GiB available workspace disk. Backfill `31562621344` still uses the prior eight-way workflow; its latest read showed 18 jobs active across the account while another corrected run remained queued. This observation does not prove a fixed GitHub plan limit.
- **Implementation and safety boundary:** `rpm-repo-backfill.yml` raises `max-parallel` from 8 to 16. Each package still receives a separate hosted runner and immutable commit, image digest, source checksum, supplemental-repository generation, artifact, and failure envelope. The existing three-attempt cache-preserving DNF policy remains the protection for the single official openEuler mirror, and build execution remains offline. Seven observed publishers failed their first payload rsync with exit 12 and the exact forced-command message `Another instance of rrsync is already accessing this directory`; none reached the ready-marker upload. The publisher now retries only that conjunction with bounded exponential delay and deterministic per-package jitter. Authentication, no-overwrite, partial-transfer, 404, checksum, and all other errors remain immediate failures. Payload still precedes `.ready`, preserving atomic server acceptance.
- **Cleanup and validation:** `make clean` isolated the run from stale outputs. Targeted RPM repository tests cover success after lock contention plus negative exit-code/message combinations; fresh `make all` passed repository and metadata validation, the full unit suite, and all three fixed golden packages. Actionlint reported no findings for the changed workflows, Bash syntax passed for the new retry helper, `ci/validate-repository.py --require-published-image` passed, and `git diff --check` was clean.
- **Next action:** publish this infrastructure-only PR through normal protected checks. After merge, start one new protected-main backfill and measure effective running jobs, official-mirror retry rate, QEMU build completion rate, publication wait/failure rate, and public RPM/SRPM growth before considering a return to twenty-way matrix concurrency.
- **Review correction:** PR `#204` review identified that `github.run_id` alone was identical for all 16 matrix calls and could synchronize retry waves. The bounded retry now hashes `run_id:package_id` again for each attempt, so independent publishers receive package-specific delay sequences while authentication, no-overwrite, checksum, HTTP, and every non-exact lock failure still fail immediately. A regression requires the per-package key and rejects a return to the shared run-only seed.

## 2026-08-11 — Codex — Per-package rpmbuild user-policy mechanism

- **Goal and evidence:** fix the shared build-identity defect demonstrated by FLAC PR `#158` exact head `2f51880df9e60ce39f0500eb009176617de88473`, Package CI run `31486213688`. Independently preserved source/build/smoke artifacts matched their Actions digests, were schema/head/target/image/source validated, and showed ten CTest cases with eight passing and only `libFLAC`/`libFLAC++` failing after 5,205.675 seconds. Their first effective error, `iterator claims file is writable when tester thinks it should not be; are you running as root?`, proves that root bypassed the read-only chmod semantics exercised by the unchanged upstream tests. This is a shared user-model defect, not RISC-V, QEMU, or a safe FLAC source/SPEC repair.
- **Compatibility boundary:** `build.user` is now an optional, schema-enumerated per-package policy accepting only `root` and `unprivileged`. Absence means `root`, preserving every existing package and root-capability suite such as ACL/attr. `needs-native-riscv` packages continue to bypass both dependency and QEMU build paths. Only an explicit package-only `unprivileged` opt-in provisions and selects the fixed `rpmbuild` identity `10001:10001`; no package directory is changed by this infrastructure commit.
- **Implementation:** the digest-pinned base image and audited BuildRequires resolution are unchanged. Dependency planning and DNF installation now state UID/GID `0:0` explicitly and record that identity. For an unprivileged opt-in, the unpublished per-run derived image receives the fixed user after dependency installation. The offline build orchestrator validates the exact 40-hex head, local derived-image tag, canonical package/work/artifact paths, read-only repository mount, and `--network none`; a root-only preparation call rejects symlink entries and hands the fresh generated work tree to `10001:10001`, then the target call verifies its effective identity and executes unchanged `scripts/build-rpm --offline`. Root policy uses the same orchestrator with UID/GID `0:0` and the historical artifact location.
- **Artifact and provenance contract:** both policies still write `rpmbuild-phase-result.json` bound to the explicit PR head, preserve the outer and internal logs, and retain RPMS/SRPMS in the existing work trees. The unprivileged path copies only three regular JSON records from its target-owned result directory into host evidence.
