## 2026-09-02 — Codex — rebuild the target RPM database through RPM's portable header stream

- **Goal and decisive evidence:** replace the incompatible raw database copy without guessing either distribution's database path and without inventing installed-package records. GitHub-hosted PR #1998 `Build CI Image` run `33641686343`, job `100286072008`, is exact-head terminal failure at `5f26bb2a787b254b2d8b120a0a304aaa287f363c`; API head, fetched pull ref, and run head agree. Its diagnostic log proves Fedora bootstrap RPM 4.20.1 used `/usr/lib/sysimage/rpm` and created a 9,965,568-byte SQLite database, while openEuler target RPM 4.18.2 evaluates `/var/lib/rpm`. Target queries against both a fresh copy and the relocated default database returned status zero but zero package records. The only live artifact, ID `9851192247`, has matching API and measured ZIP SHA-256 `2a83163312b9f49cd0b188b562760c7f5deeb330334b840f98126970e6a79d0d`; it is build evidence, not RPM/SRPM or publication evidence. Audit files are `/private/tmp/openeuler-rpmdb-rebuild-run33641686343.rlZ333`.
- **Environment and official contract:** work uses fresh branch `infra/rpmdb-target-rebuild-20260902223107` and worktree `/private/tmp/openeuler-rpmdb-target-rebuild.CH8DKS/worktree` from fetched protected `main` `577a19232e56358e9f940bed56ca7c5af341703d` on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` / `10.213.0.254`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, and approximately 38 GiB free disk. RPM upstream's exact 4.18.2 `rpmdb(8)` documentation states that `--exportdb` emits a header-list format suitable for transport to another host or database type and `--importdb` imports that format. The source document and RPM/DNF primary documentation snapshots are retained under the audit directory.
- **Implementation and trust boundary:** after the existing gpgchecked, dependency-resolved bootstrap DNF transaction, bootstrap RPM first verifies its database, exports the complete installed package headers, checksum-binds the stream, imports it into a clean round-trip database, verifies that database, and requires both the full exported header stream and sorted package identity manifest to reproduce exactly under the same RPM implementation. RPM 4.18.2's primary source implements export as `headerWrite(..., HEADER_MAGIC_YES)` over every installed header and import as `headerRead(..., HEADER_MAGIC_YES)` plus `rpmtsImportHeader`. The initial implementation attempted a deliberately strict byte-for-byte cross-version re-export gate; the live result and correction below supersede that unsupported assumption. In target stage, target RPM 4.18.2 checksum-verifies and imports the stream into a clean, non-overlapping staging database selected without a distribution path constant. It verifies the staging database and exact-compares the package identity and immutable-header-digest manifest. The evaluated target runtime path must be absent or empty; an unexpected record or file fails closed and is never deleted. Once staging is completely verified, an equal-device check, removal of only an empty runtime directory, and atomic rename place the target-generated database before manifest and low-level verification repeat. The former bootstrap database is removed only after target verification succeeds. No `--initdb`, `--justdb`, dependency bypass, signature bypass, package cache replay, empty database shortcut, hard-coded `/var/lib/rpm` or `/usr/lib/sysimage/rpm`, or second synthetic install transaction is used; imported records derive only from the original trusted DNF transaction.
- **Boundary and next action:** local static work cannot execute either target RPM or QEMU. GitHub-hosted Build CI Image must prove RPM 4.20.1 export and RPM 4.18.2 import interoperability, exact live immutable-header-digest manifest equality, and `verifydb` success before merge. PR validation must remain non-publishing; no `ci/image.lock` update is part of this change.
- **Commands and results:** validation began with `make clean` in isolated `/private/tmp/openeuler-rpmdb-target-rebuild.CH8DKS/validation/evidence`. `make validate`, all 168 repository tests, `make golden`, `make dashboard`, all 69 focused baseline/classifier/build-user/repository tests, Bash syntax, warning-level ShellCheck, Actionlint, Python compilation, `git diff --check`, and the local credential guard passed. No local target image, RPM, QEMU, or target-container build was executed.
- **First live interoperability result and corrected gate:** draft PR #2001 exact head `b089a56baa2fd7c6d6337a3484255a1778966fd8` ran Build CI Image `33645019773` on GitHub host `runnervmejwal` (`10.1.0.12`, Ubuntu kernel `6.17.0-1022-azure`, 4 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, about 88 GiB free disk, Docker 28.0.4). The gpgchecked bootstrap transaction, portable-stream checksum, target 4.18.2 import, `verifydb`, and exact package identity manifest comparison all passed. The run failed only when the target 4.18.2 re-exported stream was byte-compared with the bootstrap 4.20.1 stream. RPM documents header-list transport across hosts or database types but does not define cross-version re-export bytes as canonical. The strict manifest is therefore extended with `SHA1HEADER` and `SHA256HEADER`, which RPM 4.18.2 defines as the SHA-1 and SHA-256 digests of the package header, and remains exact across bootstrap staging, target staging, target runtime, and post-build probes. The unsupported cross-version raw-byte equality gate is removed; the original stream remains checksum-bound, the same-version bootstrap round trip remains byte-exact, and target `verifydb` remains mandatory. Artifact ID `9852507488` has API digest `sha256:c86e48ee5f70bcf4dbaaf2f03dc3b9970a5156ab6a55d559e54a9aaf31a90485`; downloaded evidence is `/private/tmp/pr2001-artifact.yUsD1p`, and failure logs are `/private/tmp/pr2001-failure-audit.bCQtie`. This run published nothing.

The exact evidence is `/private/tmp/pr2001-golden-results.IsiT1k`. PR #1060 exact head `e3cf6b0a2d785b3ba24c6ecb9c8fed673abf41d6` likewise stopped before rpmbuild on the empty locked-image baseline and produced no RPM, SRPM, or publication evidence. GEOS #285 run `33634913667` at `f4aac3f8ba2f1e5db1a2f57759466554521fe0a0` and diamond #1922 run `33634962291` at `391d242d385814e73b3c3196f37f57f62ebed977` remain in their 180-minute build windows with no terminal verdict; audit evidence is `/private/tmp/openeuler-geos-diamond-current-audit.vvI0Pr`.
- **Successful semantic-header canary and format hardening:** exact head `40d7c12dbfcf31dcd7ef0d218183633f4c46e76c` passed Build CI Image run `33646413132`: target import and `verifydb`, the 278-row embedded/live header-digest manifest, and the RVA23 Zba/Zbb/Zbs probe all passed. Artifact `9853101634` has matching API and measured ZIP digest `sha256:eecc83d68e3a759d4f1521cc0e16bf01bb2e4bda6110be790c9d94ff0c37cbee`; evidence is `/private/tmp/pr2001-success-artifact.gvZHeO` and `/private/tmp/openeuler-pr2001-workflows.k8tRc1`. All 278 SHA-1/SHA-256 header fields were valid 40/64-character lowercase hexadecimal values. The legacy `SIGPGP` query field was `(none)` for all 278 records and is therefore removed rather than misrepresented as signature evidence; transaction trust remains the original gpgchecked DNF install. The manifest helper now fails closed unless every row has exactly five fields and both immutable-header digests have the required format. The run explicitly says GHCR was not modified; its local OCI digest is not publication evidence.

## 2026-09-03 — Codex — separate the networkless RPM preflight from dependency egress

- **Goal, environment, and acceptance target:** unblock Golden Evaluation and package CI after the verified target RPM database image reached protected main. Work uses isolated branch `infra/dependency-network-transition-20260902234207` and worktree `/private/tmp/openeuler-deps-network-fix.KS7sjF/worktree` from exact protected `main` `8222b021855db899fff8f21c1a9a7dd6f8a4bf6f` on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` / `10.213.0.254`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, and approximately 38 GiB free disk. The target remains openEuler 24.03 LTS SP3 `riscv64`/RVA23. Acceptance requires a real Golden run to pass the live 278-package RPM baseline, enter dependency installation without Docker's forbidden `none`-to-bridge transition, and retain the existing source checksum and DNF repository trust boundaries.
- **Observed protected-main evidence:** RPM database repair PR #2001 passed final Build CI Image run `33647653592` and squash-merged as `8858f58707c153cf97a979bb18eadd8c7c01bc07`. Protected-main image run `33648480917` published and anonymously verified `ghcr.io/yinjiayi/openeuler-riscv64-rpmbuild@sha256:df0ed12bc2db3d8256363134ab7120ac09ec09054d67c34516dacefaea1e0654`; its artifact `9853908939` has matching API and measured ZIP SHA-256 `635d913437cda1d983d53bfebf784d075b9323439022c55d516b18fde121660a`. Image-lock PR #2002 then passed the six required package contexts and squash-merged as current base `8222b021855db899fff8f21c1a9a7dd6f8a4bf6f`. Main Golden run `33649805964` resolved and probed that exact image, passed repository resolution and the live RPM baseline, then both buildable goldens failed at `prepare-build-deps.py` when Docker rejected `docker network connect bridge` for a running container created with private `--network none`. This is an infrastructure state-machine error; no golden package rpmbuild, RPM, SRPM, or publication resulted.
- **Implementation and evidence contract:** the immutable base image's RPM manifest is now queried in a dedicated one-shot, read-only RISC-V container that uses `--pull never` and remains in Docker network mode `none` for its whole lifetime. Empty, malformed, or anchor-incomplete baselines therefore fail before any long-lived container or egress network exists. Only a passed baseline and non-empty audited BuildRequires list create a uniquely named, session-labelled user-defined bridge and a long-lived dependency container directly attached to it; network and container inspection must agree on the full IDs, labels, driver, scope, external-routing policy, and exclusive endpoint before the atomic evidence record authorizes DNF. After a successful transaction the bridge is disconnected, verified empty from both sides, and removed before provisioning, manifest capture, cache cleanup, and image commit. Empty dependency lists create the long-lived container directly in `none` mode, record a successful network-not-required state, execute no DNF argv, and create no bridge. All runtime container operations and ordinary cleanup use the captured full container ID; cleanup continues across individual Docker command exceptions. Existing dedicated-runner recovery still removes fully identified managed dependency containers and then unused custom networks after an externally forced termination; disposable GitHub-hosted runners do not persist them across jobs.
- **Local validation boundary and next action:** this host has no Docker CLI and no local target build, RPM build, QEMU run, image publication, supplemental repository publication, or quarantined fleet access was performed. The first fresh complete suite ran 186 tests and exposed one pre-existing protected-main inconsistency rather than a new network test failure: `ops/actions-runner-fleet/cleanup-image.lock` still named old digest `c9d8e675...` after #2002 locked `df0ed12b...`. This would prevent the dedicated-runner recovery helper from recognizing an interrupted dependency container created from the current image, so the infrastructure change synchronizes that existing required mirror to the already anonymously verified digest. Independent review then identified the successful-create/malformed-stdout cleanup edge; exact name plus session/managed labels now safely recover a full object ID, while identity mismatch refuses deletion. The final clean round in `/private/tmp/openeuler-deps-network-fix.KS7sjF/validation-final-3` passed metadata/index/repository validation, all 188 tests, Golden materialization, Dashboard generation, Python compilation, 21 focused RPM/network tests, and `git diff --check`. The exact Docker reference snapshots reviewed for `network create`, connect/disconnect, and bridge semantics are under `/private/tmp/docker-network-docs.Gsi9XB`; independent review evidence is under `/private/tmp/openeuler-docker-network-state-audit.vS3U5t` and `/private/tmp/builddeps-network-test-design.DJRav5`. Submit this as a separate infrastructure PR only after the credential guard passes. Merge only after GitHub-hosted Golden proves the real lifecycle; then update and rerun unchanged gtk4-layer-shell #1060 as a package canary. GEOS #285 has now failed only by exhausting the validated QEMU build budget after normal compilation reached about 79%, while diamond #1922 reached `%check` and failed two of 39 tests on a `ThreadPool` null assertion that requires a native-RISC-V comparison before any test or source change. Repository resolution succeeded against immutable generation `sirocco-dc29f3c757e87a63408d4db30c7c0aca980e16d4-33327494617-1`, binding `riscv64` repomd SHA-256 `9bbd935424d976f401aa1fe9801d2b9c612179ee72d8a99026c18103c6692f13` and source repomd SHA-256 `9048cff765e925459b9666eb864b07f426e0d555b1bcb458ce110f4df067ce52`. ## 2026-09-03 — Codex — classify effective test timeouts without zero-count noise

- **Goal, environment, and scope:** repair the shared failure-summary tooling on isolated detached worktree `/private/tmp/openeuler-classifier-fix.wyHt0l/worktree` at exact fetched protected `main` `606fad41537d38b70ca5ec1727f82cb8ca7d23cb`. Work ran on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, and approximately 38 GiB free disk. The fixed target remains openEuler 24.03 LTS SP3 `riscv64`/RVA23, but this round changed and statically tested host-side Python only; no local RPM, QEMU, target Docker build, runner-fleet access, publication, commit, push, PR, or other remote mutation occurred.
- **Implementation and evidence boundary:** `scripts/build-rpm:first_error` now rejects prefixed and unprefixed Automake/Meson zero `FAIL`, `ERROR`, and `TIMEOUT` counters plus CTest's zero-failed summary, collects all effective candidates by byte offset, and returns the earliest log occurrence. Its timeout candidate accepts explicit `timed out`/`time out` diagnostics and duration-bearing result rows while ignoring configuration identifiers and ordinary `timeout=` options. `scripts/classify-failure` applies the same zero-count suppression and adds a high-confidence `upstream-build` test-timeout rule after native/QEMU/infrastructure/RISC-V precedence but before the generic RPM wrapper. Timeout-only evidence remains `repairable_locally=false` and `source_patch_allowed=false`; one QEMU test timeout alone does not prove either a package defect or a native-RISC-V requirement.
- **Exact artifact replay:** the unchanged 633,306-byte `rpmbuild-internal.log` from gtk4-layer-shell run `33660703308` at package head `369d2454be93de94d68f3d803e93dab02a237c15` now yields `lock-test-multiple-monitors time out (After 120 seconds)` instead of Meson's later `Fail: 0`. Reclassification of the original phase result plus full log yields high-confidence `upstream-build`, recommended state `failed`, and both repair/source-patch flags false; the generic `Bad exit status` wrapper no longer erases the earlier test timeout. One initial ad hoc `runpy` replay omitted `PYTHONPATH=scripts` and failed to import `_lib`; the corrected invocation produced the result above, while the tool CLI replay and all automated tests were unaffected.
- **Cleanup, commands, and results:** authoritative final validation under `/private/tmp/openeuler-classifier-validation-final.qVQkkg` began with `make clean`; `make validate`, all 195 repository tests, `make golden`, `make dashboard`, 14 focused build/classifier tests, exact-artifact replay, Python compilation, and `git diff --check` passed. Regression coverage includes the exact Meson sequence, zero-only summaries, RPM-wrapper fallback, a real positive failure before a later timeout, timeout-like configuration text, zero-failed CTest output, classifier precedence, confidence, disposition, and preservation of existing RISC-V and infrastructure routing.
- **Next action:** review this as a separate infrastructure change and exercise the classifier against another real Meson or CTest timeout before merge. Do not use the corrected category to auto-edit package sources; inspect the exact timeout evidence first and require independent native-RISC-V evidence before changing route policy.

## 2026-09-03 — Codex — preserve chronological package failures and reject benign star banners

- **Goal, environment, and scope:** resolve the two remaining P2 review findings on PR #2004 without changing its remote state: an earlier effective package failure must not be overwritten by a later timeout category, and a decorative line containing `***` must not become the RPM wrapper's first effective error. Work used fresh isolated worktree `/private/tmp/repair-pr2004-classifier-p2.YO6ZPm/worktree` at the exact PR head `169b25a241b3849a4a26ac8d47dee022cd20fcfc` on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` / `10.213.0.254`, Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, and about 35 GiB free disk. - **Implementation contract:** target-, environment-, dependency-, and architecture-significant rules retain their established fail-closed precedence. Ordinary `spec-packaging` and `upstream-build` candidates are now compared by their first effective byte position so a later high-confidence timeout cannot replace an earlier real `FAIL:`. The generic `Bad exit status from /var/tmp/rpm-tmp` summary remains fallback evidence because structured result fields precede the detailed log. Embedded zero counters such as `rpmbuild failed with exit code 1: Fail: 0` are ignored. Generic tool-error matching now requires an actual `compiler:`, `linker:`, `make:`, `gmake:`, `ninja:`, or `meson.build:...: ERROR` form, avoiding command-line filenames such as `meson-generated... -Werror`. `scripts/build-rpm:first_error` accepts only GNU make/gmake diagnostic forms around `***`; banners such as `*** Configuration summary ***` are ignored while `gmake[2]: *** ... Error 2` remains evidence.
- **Focused and real-artifact evidence:** three new focused regressions cover an earlier `FAIL:` followed by a Meson timeout and RPM wrapper, a benign star banner followed by the real failure, and preservation of a genuine GNU make error. The existing timeout-versus-wrapper test now also reproduces the structured `Fail: 0` prefix and benign `meson-generated... -Werror` command line. All 17 focused build/classifier tests pass. Replaying the unchanged 633,306-byte gtk4-layer-shell run `33660703308` phase result and full RPM log yields high-confidence `upstream-build`, recommended state `failed`, and both repair/source-patch permissions false, with `lock-test-multiple-monitors time out (After 120 seconds)` as the first evidence. The first replay after the chronological change exposed the pre-existing broad tool-error false positive and returned repairable `upstream-build`; the tightened tool form and embedded-zero filter above corrected it, and the exact replay then passed all assertions. Evidence is under `/private/tmp/repair-pr2004-classifier-p2.YO6ZPm/evidence`.
- **Cleanup, commands, and results:** validation began with `make clean`; `make validate`, all 198 repository tests, `make golden`, `make dashboard`, the 17 focused tests, exact-artifact replay assertions, Actionlint, Python compilation, and `git diff --check` pass. Warning-level ShellCheck over every tracked `*.sh` exits zero but reports existing SC2034/SC2046/SC2154 findings in untouched runner-fleet and package smoke scripts; none is introduced by these Python-only changes. Final generated metadata, package-index, repository, Golden, and Dashboard artifacts remain repository-valid.
- **Next action and evidence boundary:** obtain independent review of the chronological/fallback distinction, then run the unchanged exact head through GitHub-hosted PR checks. Merge only if Package CI, Golden Evaluation, and policy checks are terminal success at that exact head. The local evidence proves parser behavior and repository consistency only; it does not prove a target RPM build or authorize resolving either review thread.

## 2026-09-03 — Codex — close the Auto-merge immediate-completion race

- **Goal, environment, and isolation:** make the single-package Auto-merge transaction fail closed when all pre-existing package checks are already successful, without changing any package. Work used detached isolated worktree `/private/tmp/automerge-race-fix.pqVAba/worktree` at exact protected-main commit `14e4d985378c05cd4cccde4748ec9a98408452b9` on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` / `10.213.0.254`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, and approximately 38 GiB free disk. Python 3.9.6, Git 2.50.1, Actionlint 1.7.12, and ShellCheck 0.11.0 were available. Final generated validation output was isolated under `/private/tmp/automerge-race-fix.pqVAba/validation-final`; `make clean` removed that exact directory before the complete final evidence round. No commit, push, PR, label, ruleset write, workflow rerun, package build, publication, runner login, or fleet recovery was performed.
- **Observed race and exact evidence:** Auto Merge Policy run `33669948581` at package head `1805c5d0670683252419048fa171d287d829b89d` disarmed and evaluated PR #1060 successfully, then invoked `gh pr merge --auto` after all six existing required checks had already passed. GitHub immediately merged the PR at `2026-09-02T18:53:20Z` as protected-main commit `14e4d985378c05cd4cccde4748ec9a98408452b9`; the arming step subsequently failed at `18:53:23Z` because its post-arm readback expected a non-null Auto-merge request. The EXIT rollback then saw the merged PR's `auto_merge=null` and incorrectly accepted that field alone as proof of disarm even though state was `closed`, `merged=true`, and `merged_at` was non-null. Read-only run JSON and failed log are `/private/tmp/automerge-race-fix.pqVAba/run-33669948581.json` and `run-33669948581-failed.log`, SHA-256 `dcd2b4ea7e55d327dbf68a8b3093d847535588e04b81c8a0d8ec46d5ff76d644` and `3170be6759484e4c6fb4d6412844ab829dc7818edc99f97b7fd8cee140f8e239`.
- **Implementation and state contract:** `.github/rulesets/main.json` requires the established Auto Merge Policy `configure` context after the six package contexts. That check stays pending while its own disarm/evaluate/arm/readback transaction runs, preventing an already-green PR from completing during `gh pr merge --auto`; only the successful job conclusion can release the merge. Reusing the established context avoids renaming the check on already-open exact heads. Mandatory-disarm, pre-arm, post-arm, and rollback readbacks bind repository, exact event head/base, `state=open`, `merged=false`, and `merged_at=null`, as well as the expected Auto-merge field. An already merged, closed, changed-lease, or cross-repository PR can no longer satisfy rollback proof. `ci/configure-github.sh` reads back the applied ruleset by exact ID, projects out only API-stable policy fields, and exact-compares them with the committed configuration. A readback failure or mismatch attempts to restore the exact preceding policy and verifies that restoration; an unverifiable rollback is reported as an urgent unknown live state. Repository validation and README are synchronized with the context and accurately distinguish blocking automatic merge from a separately authorized manual maintainer merge.
- **Validation and anomaly:** after the first clean validation correctly exposed one stale static marker that still expected the replaced pre-arm shell assertion, the validator was updated to require the new state predicates and the complete clean round passed: metadata, 151,852-entry package index, repository validation, all 190 unit tests, Golden materialization, and Dashboard generation. The final focused Auto-merge suite passed 13/13; Actionlint over all workflows, warning-level ShellCheck, Bash/Python/JSON syntax, configuration dry-run, `git diff --check`, and authenticated local credential guard also passed. Dry-run evidence is `/private/tmp/automerge-race-fix.pqVAba/configure-dry-run-final.json`, SHA-256 `5c3909babe90192ee619256dad032863765d23e186006d1189a1612c5743f426`. These local checks do not prove live GitHub ruleset application or a successful merge transition.
- **Concurrent publication blocker and next action:** read-only GitHub API evidence at this checkpoint reports exactly 50 registered `oe-rva23-qemu-201` through `oe-rva23-qemu-250` runners, with 0 online and 50 offline; `/private/tmp/automerge-race-fix.pqVAba/runners.json` has SHA-256 `70f9cf2cf07feaa5ffa7ac2e6f9f9c0b19eefe7ebf21d355ffd1beb77434dbce`. Protected-main Package CI run `33670215585` at `14e4d985...` had reached `rpmbuild-riscv64` but remained queued; its snapshot SHA-256 is `3a3b14d02ffa981bd1c89070e87949e092f368594060676d02550cac7fbde7dc`. The quarantined fleet was not accessed or recovered, so RPM/SRPM publication remains blocked and no publication success is claimed. Submit this infrastructure-only repair under manual control. After it reaches protected main, backfill and verify the six remaining open-PR `configure` gaps before applying the ruleset; require the exact readback to contain `configure`, and then exercise one eligible single-package canary. Acceptance requires the arming command to leave the PR open with non-null Auto-merge while the job is in progress, the job itself to succeed, and only then GitHub to merge; fleet recovery is a separate authorized milestone.

- **Independent review, migration audit, and dynamic proof:** review of PR #2006 exact head `e6447f67664bd4c3516a8b0723e27c3e2526df77` found that a newly named required context would strand existing heads and that repeated workflow predicates had only static text coverage. A stable two-pass GraphQL audit at 2026-09-02T20:47:15Z–20:48:48Z checked all 1,433 open PR exact heads twice with zero head drift or context-page overflow. Exactly 1,426 heads carried `configure`; all 1,526 occurrences came only from the Auto Merge Policy workflow and GitHub Actions, with no other-workflow or provenance collision. Seven heads lacked it: #448, #449, #1362, #1992, #1993, #1994, and #2006; after this PR receives its replacement-head run, six historical gaps require an audited label-event backfill before ruleset application. Evidence is `/private/tmp/open-pr-configure-context-audit.yNcOgl`, whose 41-file manifest recomputes with zero checksum failures. The post-checkout rollback, pre-arm, and post-arm paths now share executable `ci/prove-auto-merge-state.py`; fake-CLI tests cover valid disabled/enabled state, API failure, malformed shape, missing fields, closed/merged/timestamped state, and head/base repository or SHA drift. Mandatory disarm deliberately remains inline before checkout so checkout failure cannot leave an old arm active. No live ruleset write or backfill was performed during this review round.

## 2026-09-03 — Codex — gate the required-context migration before any write

- **Goal and implementation:** turn the one-time `configure` coverage study into a reusable fail-closed gate. New executable `ci/audit-required-context.py` accepts repository, context, expected workflow/app provenance, and an evidence output. It fully paginates open PR exact heads and their first 100 combined contexts, repeats the complete PR/head scan, and exits nonzero on set/head drift, incomplete or overflowing context pages, missing context, legacy `StatusContext`, wrong provenance, malformed API data, or duplicate PR identity. Multiple historical CheckRuns from the same trusted workflow/app remain visible but do not become a false provenance failure; the audit proves presence and origin, not successful conclusion.
- **Configuration ordering and rollback:** `ci/configure-github.sh --apply` runs the audit for `configure` / `Auto Merge Policy` / `github-actions` after authenticated repository identity validation but before its first remote write. `--dry-run` exits before the audit and remains local-only. The ruleset update separately snapshots the preceding accepted policy, exact-compares the new API readback, and attempts an exact verified restoration on update/readback/mismatch failure; an uncertain create or unverifiable restore stops with an explicit live-state warning.
- **Live preflight and acceptance boundary:** the new gate reproduced the stable 1,433-head snapshot and intentionally failed on the same seven exact-head gaps, with zero wrong-provenance, `StatusContext`, overflow, head mismatch, or changed-head cases. JSON evidence `/private/tmp/pr2006-required-context-live-e6447f.json` has SHA-256 `83b11652dab6e72744e808292fb610ab97b59d73636c8cd5da05b9ea8262afab`. This proves that current configuration remains blocked; it does not authorize or claim a ruleset write. After #2006 leaves the old snapshot, create genuine workflow checks on the six listed legacy heads, rerun the gate to stable zero gaps, and only then apply and exact-read back the ruleset.
- **Rollout-race review and correction:** final review found that the interval between merging this workflow and enabling the new required context could still reproduce the #1060 immediate-merge race during the very label events used for backfill. The protected-base workflow now reads the uniquely named live ruleset immediately before arming and requires an active branch target covering `~DEFAULT_BRANCH` with exactly one `configure` required context. Absence or inactive rollout state records a successful no-op and leaves Auto-merge disarmed; ambiguous identity, malformed data, or API failure fails closed. Enabled Auto-merge proof now also requires an object whose `merge_method` is exactly `squash`, rather than accepting any non-null value.
- **Configuration critical section and rollback proof:** the apply path retains its before-any-write migration audit and repeats the complete stable audit immediately before ruleset mutation. Created-rule deletion is verified by a successful paginated listing that proves the exact numeric ID absent; arbitrary GET failure is no longer accepted as deletion proof. A failed or malformed create response attempts to discover exactly one new same-name ID relative to the pre-create ID set and remove it, otherwise reports unknown live state for human inspection. Focused fake-CLI coverage exercises active/inactive/absent/ambiguous/malformed/API-error activation states and wrong Auto-merge object/method shapes. No repository configuration was written during these corrections.
- **Default-branch and malformed-response closure:** a second review identified that filtering the workflow itself to `main` would miss the exact retarget event that must disarm a previously armed PR. The workflow therefore keeps all target branches in scope for mandatory disarm, then binds live PR head/base SHA/base ref and repository default branch before checkout. Non-default targets stop successfully while disarmed; checkout, evaluation, activation, and arming are all gated on that proof. The live activation helper now exact-compares the protected branch conditions, requires empty exclusions and bypass actors, and rejects drift. Create responses with invalid JSON or scalar shapes enter discovery rollback, mutated exact readbacks with invalid identity or policy shape enter exact rollback, and deletion is explicitly reported unverified when its confirming list request fails.
- **Final clean validation:** after the default-branch and malformed-response corrections, a fresh `make clean`, `make validate`, `make test` (225/225), `make golden`, and `make dashboard` completed successfully. The focused Auto-merge, state-proof, activation, context-audit, and ruleset state-machine suites passed 38/38; Actionlint, Bash syntax, error-level ShellCheck, Python compilation, configuration dry-run, and `git diff --check` also passed. This is local policy evidence only: exact-head hosted checks, legacy-head backfill, live ruleset application/readback, canary merge ordering, and RPM publication remain pending.

## 2026-09-03 — Codex — prove caller-relative ruleset bypass safety

- **Goal, environment, and observed failure:** restore safe Auto-merge rollout progress after eligible pstack PR #1362 run `33689321234` failed its activation step at exact head `c6a5178b3bd7d36a0e64f3b41aeebe5efabc44fd`. The job's `GITHUB_TOKEN` could uniquely list and read ruleset `20579949`, but GitHub omitted the optional `bypass_actors` field; the previous helper treated that permission-dependent omission as malformed before noticing that the live ruleset still lacked `configure`. Work used isolated branch/worktree `infra/automerge-ruleset-attestation` at protected `main` `fd49c267b6a646ab73d7ab8614f0441d69757312` on `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` / `10.213.0.254`, macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5 arm64, 8 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, and approximately 29 GiB free disk. Git was 2.50.1, GitHub CLI 2.97.0, and Python 3.9.6. No package build, QEMU run, runner-fleet access, repository publication, ruleset mutation, commit, push, or PR mutation occurred during implementation.
- **Live evidence and security definition:** an administrator-authenticated exact read shows the same ruleset is repository-owned, active, scoped to the default branch, has `bypass_actors=[]`, and reports `current_user_can_bypass="never"`; it still contains only the former six required contexts. A **caller-relative bypass proof** means the bearer token that will run `gh pr merge --auto` is reported by GitHub as unable to bypass the live ruleset. It does not claim that a redacted global bypass list is empty. This distinction permits a truthful rollout no-op when `configure` is absent while continuing to fail closed if the caller-bypass field is missing or anything other than the exact `never` enum.
- **Implementation contract:** the activation helper now checks required-context absence before permission-dependent global fields, so pre-activation rollout remains a successful disarmed no-op. Once `configure` exists, the exact response must be owned by the target repository, have a nonempty node identity, match the complete visible committed policy, pin the context to GitHub Actions App integration ID `15368`, and report `current_user_can_bypass="never"`. A visible bypass list must be exactly empty; an omitted list is recorded as `not-returned` with `bypass_actors_verified_empty=false`, without guessing why. The arming transaction repeats this live proof immediately before `gh pr merge --auto`, closing ruleset drift between the earlier evaluation step and the mutation. The administrator apply/readback path no longer converts a missing bypass or branch-condition field into an empty list, binds the exact ruleset ID/name/repository source at previous, applied, and restored readbacks, and preserves each required context's integration ID.
- **Stale-base Auto-merge incident:** julius-speech PR #1899 exact head `c8694d6f6b5cdd818d0a43fb89c29fb6d01b6e8a` had a clean single-package diff and Package CI run `33688014171` succeeded across all required package gates; 8/8 downloaded artifact digests matched and the final result bound three RPMs plus one SRPM and installed smoke to that head. Publication was skipped. After the credential guard passed, converting the draft to ready triggered the workflow from stale PR base `14e4d985378c05cd4cccde4748ec9a98408452b9`. That historical workflow armed Auto-merge without the newer barrier and GitHub immediately squash-merged the PR as `fc7efd6b189b829cf06aded57a270aa32906385e`; run `33690938933` then failed its post-arm assertion because the PR was already merged. No explicit merge command was issued after the state change, and the branch was automatically deleted. Repeated API checks found no protected-main workflow for `fc7efd6b...`, so the successful PR artifacts are not publication and no RPM/SRPM URL is claimed. Evidence is under `/private/tmp/pr1899-final-audit.AbGXOC` and `/private/tmp/automerge-attestation.FZSCtQ/pr1899-auto-merge-failed.log`.
- **Freshness closure and concurrent package evidence:** before checkout, a repository-file-independent inline `gh`/`jq` gate requires the event base SHA to equal the current protected `main` ref; after checkout, new executable `ci/prove-default-branch-head.py` repeats that proof immediately before arming. PR #2008's first hosted run `33692245016` safely exposed why this split is required: its initial implementation tried to execute the helper before checkout, returned 127 because the file was not present, skipped every later phase, and left Auto-merge off. API failure, malformed ref data, a non-default target, and a stale base cannot reach the arming command. The ruleset also enables strict required-status checks, so GitHub itself requires the PR to remain current at the final merge decision; a later `main` advance blocks completion until the branch is refreshed and its new head re-runs the protected checks. Xde-session PR #1873 advanced by ordinary exact-head HTTPS fast-forward to Release 10 head `c7038c09af5f5b203e92930bb99b5c3684ad01d8`, where run `33689651155` proved `gtk2-devel` fixed the prior layer but exposed mandatory `pkgconfig(unique-1.0)` with no provider in the checksum-bound official or supplemental metadata, managed packages, inventory, or open PRs. It is now truthfully labeled `needs-human` and `failure:dependency`, `repair-queued` is absent, and Auto-merge is off. Xoreos #1875 exact head `406fe3b17539ccd3e05737e382da9e0e127a5178` built and installed successfully before all 82 CTest entries were `Not Run`: upstream marks test binaries `EXCLUDE_FROM_ALL`, so the package must invoke its real `check` target. A package-only Release 4 candidate passed 188 local repository tests and full static validation at `/private/tmp/repair-pr1875-xoreos-r4.kK0TnS/worktree`; it remains unpushed until the stale-base Auto-merge repair reaches protected main.
- **Validation, anomaly, and next action:** the focused activation, configuration-state-machine, freshness, and workflow suite passes 41/41, covering a not-returned bypass response only with caller `never`, every other caller state as hard failure, explicit nonempty or malformed global bypass, wrong source/type/node identity, required-context integration spoofing or duplication, exact policy drift, rollout absence, stale/non-default base, missing administrator bypass readback, rollback after integration drift, strict up-to-date enforcement, and the ordered repeated pre-arm proofs. A clean `make validate`, all 233 repository tests, `make golden`, `make dashboard`, Actionlint, error-level ShellCheck, Python/Bash syntax, configuration dry-run, `git diff --check`, and the authenticated credential guard pass. The first ad hoc live-helper shell wrapper used zsh's reserved read-only variable name `status` and stopped before printing the result; the corrected wrapper used `proof_rc` and proved the current live ruleset is the intended exit-3 no-op because `configure` occurs zero times. Independent review found no remaining P1/P2 correctness or security issue. Hosted exact-head checks remain pending. After this infrastructure PR reaches protected main, retrigger #1362 to fill its exact-head `configure` gap, rerun the two-pass all-open-PR migration audit to zero gaps, and only then apply plus exact-read back the ruleset. A single eligible package canary must prove that the workflow token sees `current_user_can_bypass="never"`, remains open while `configure` is pending, and merges only after that job succeeds. Publication remains separate and requires matching immutable protected-main upload evidence.

## 2026-09-03 — Codex — disable token-originated Auto-merge and preserve protected-main publication events

- **Goal and hypothesis:** stop repository automation from arming GitHub Auto-merge. A merge whose Auto-merge request was enabled by the repository `GITHUB_TOKEN` is attributed to `github-actions[bot]`; GitHub then suppresses the resulting push-triggered workflows. Acceptance requires the checked-in repository setting `allow_auto_merge=false`, continued exact-head eligibility reporting through the established required `configure` context, no `gh pr merge --auto` in the policy or daily-update application path, and a final proof that the pull request remains open, unmerged, lease-bound, and disarmed. A separately authorized maintainer squash merge remains the only normal merge route.
- **Environment and isolation:** implementation used new worktree `/private/tmp/openeuler-disable-automerge.8Y4Exi/worktree`, branch `fix-disable-repo-auto-merge-local-openeuler-disable-automerge.8Y4Exi`, based on exact remote `main` `867a0e29053d4f5fe00774258ed1893a3ab8fada`. Host `ASKYCN-Macmini.local` / `10.213.0.254` ran macOS 26.5 / Darwin 25.5.0 arm64 with 8 CPUs, 16 GiB RAM, approximately 8.3 GiB free/inactive/speculative memory, and 27 GiB free disk. Tooling was Python 3.9.6, Git 2.50.1, GitHub CLI 2.97.0, GNU Make 3.81, Actionlint 1.7.12, and ShellCheck 0.11.0. `make clean` removed only this worktree's ignored `work/local-validation` outputs before evidence-producing runs; no dirty root worktree was touched.
- **Exact #1884 canary:** parlatype PR #1884 exact head `bcdf20213413c9ef1273dd1561ffd0b3a9ea4825` completed Package CI run `33698640364` successfully, including the `riscv64` RPM build, all 8 Meson tests, installed-RPM smoke, and exact artifact checks; its pull-request publication job was correctly skipped. Auto Merge Policy run `33700679912` then evaluated the package eligible and executed `gh pr merge --auto --squash` with `github-actions[bot]`. GitHub squash-merged it at `2026-09-03T00:43:37Z` as protected-main commit `867a0e29053d4f5fe00774258ed1893a3ab8fada`. An exact-head Actions API read after the merge returned `total_count=0` for that main commit: no protected-main Package CI ran, so no RPM/SRPM publication occurred and no repository product URL is claimed. Evidence is under `/private/tmp/automerge-disable-policy-evidence.Soyate`; SHA-256 values are `cd0562e1fe31b976feaa9b2e685ae9b0b74e3028044bd680e492be4b863a133f` for the PR API document, `fbe78897e7694cbecdd91bbbfcc7cb85ae8a6f43e68b1f5ee67cb2bb5a5e71c8` for the arming log, and `a2790a384d7d281e7395679000c35d27768d89dbd7052f725b8f4688beb59915` for the zero-run main response.
- **Emergency bulk disarm evidence:** the first complete live query found exactly 1,389 open pull requests with non-null `autoMergeRequest`. PR #1994 at exact head `2dfc45d214ff646b145bcbfc4961255e5cc71a09` was the single-operation GraphQL canary; its immediate API readback was null, leaving 1,388 requests in `/private/tmp/automerge-disarm-v8t9if/armed-before.json` (SHA-256 `282422daf18d1325e4711b32743e6933f3764374e10eabf932697995fdc96a3e`). The first attempted 50-alias response timed out or returned GraphQL complexity errors after GitHub had asynchronously applied only some mutations, so response-level success counts from that request are not used as evidence. Repeated exact live snapshots and bounded retries continued until a fresh list of all 1,430 open PRs reported zero non-null Auto-merge requests. The final snapshot `/private/tmp/automerge-disable-policy-evidence.Soyate/open-prs-final.json` has SHA-256 `241b619d04f5a7376fb8ed9a60ed1dc4201848ed1424288c7a4baedc67714d57`; #1994 still reads open at the same head with `autoMergeRequest=null`. Repository API readback also reports `allow_auto_merge=false`. These remote mitigations preceded this local code change; this implementation performed no remote write.
- **Implementation:** `.github/repository-settings.json` and `ci/configure-github.sh` now require disabled Auto-merge and exact-read back all merge settings. The Auto Merge Policy keeps its established `configure` job, mandatory pre-checkout disarm, current-default-base binding, immutable protected-base evaluator, complete file-list eligibility decision, and exact final disabled-state proof, but removes the activation/arming/rollback transaction and drops `contents: write`. The daily update applier, catalog-snapshot workflow, and CI-image digest-lock workflow still create their intended PRs and bridge required checks, but each reports that an explicit maintainer squash merge is required instead of arming the PR. Repository validation and focused tests scan workflow, CI, and script automation and reject any return of a `gh pr merge` Auto-merge/squash command, enabled-state proof, arming step, or contents-write permission while preserving the existing policy and proof helper coverage. README defines the evaluation-only merge policy and the event-origin/publication boundary.
- **Validation result and evidence boundary:** after the final repository-wide automation scan was added, a fresh `make clean`, `make validate`, `make test` (235/235), `make golden`, and `make dashboard` all passed. The focused Auto Merge Policy and GitHub configurator suites passed 23/23; configuration dry-run proved `allow_auto_merge=false` with no writes, and Actionlint, Bash/Python syntax, error-level ShellCheck, `git diff --check`, plus the authenticated local credential guard passed. Generated evidence and logs are under `/private/tmp/automerge-disable-policy-evidence.Soyate/validation`. These local tests do not themselves prove a future maintainer-originated merge or protected-main publication. No local RPM/QEMU build, commit, push, PR mutation, merge, workflow dispatch, runner access, or repository publication occurred in this implementation round.
- **Next action:** submit this as an infrastructure-only PR under explicit maintainer control. After its exact-head hosted checks pass, merge it with a direct maintainer squash operation, confirm the resulting protected-main Package CI starts for that exact commit, and require matching build, installed smoke, upload, and immutable-generation verification before calling the package published. Keep all open PRs disarmed; never reconstruct RPM/SRPM links from pull-request artifacts.
